705→def resolve_provider_credentials(
   706→    provider: str,
   707→    model: str,
   708→) -> Optional[dict]:
   709→    """Resolve a provider+model into a complete credentials bundle.
   710→
   711→    Thin wrapper around :func:`resolve_provider_client` that also derives
   712→    ``api_mode`` from the provider name.  Used by every code path that
   713→    needs to construct or switch to a new provider: fallback activation,
   714→    the ``switch_model`` tool, and per-task delegation overrides.
   715→
   716→    Returns a dict on success::
   717→
   718→        {
   719→            "provider": "openrouter",
   720→            "model": "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6",
   721→            "base_url": "https://...",
   722→            "api_key": "sk-...",
   723→            "api_mode": "chat_completions",
   724→            "client": <OpenAI>,       # ready-to-use client object
   725→        }
   726→
   727→    Returns ``None`` if the provider is not configured (no API key).
   728→    """
   729→    client, _ = resolve_provider_client(provider, model=model, raw_codex=True)
   730→    if client is None:
   731→        return None
   732→
   733→    api_mode = "chat_completions"
   734→    if provider == "openai-codex":
   735→        api_mode = "codex_responses"
   736→    elif provider == "anthropic":
   737→        api_mode = "anthropic_messages"
   738→
   739→    return {
   740→        "provider": provider,
   741→        "model": model,
   742→        "base_url": str(client.base_url),
   743→        "api_key": client.api_key,
   744→        "api_mode": api_mode,
   745→        "client": client,
   746→    }
   747→
   748→
   749→# ── Public API ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   750→
   751→def get_text_auxiliary_client(task: str = "") -> Tuple[Optional[OpenAI], Optional[str]]:
   752→    """Return (client, default_model_slug) for text-only auxiliary tasks.
   753→
   754→    Args:
   755→        task: Optional task name ("compression", "web_extract") to check
   756→              for a task-specific provider override.
   757→
   758→    Callers may override the returned model with a per-task env var
   759→    (e.g. CONTEXT_COMPRESSION_MODEL, AUXILIARY_WEB_EXTRACT_MODEL).
   760→    """
   761→    forced = _get_auxiliary_provider(task)
   762→    if forced != "auto":
   763→        return resolve_provider_client(forced)
   764→    return resolve_provider_client("auto")
   765→
   766→
   767→def get_async_text_auxiliary_client(task: str = ""):
   768→    """Return (async_client, model_slug) for async consumers.
   769→
   770→    For standard providers returns (AsyncOpenAI, model). For Codex returns
   771→    (AsyncCodexAuxiliaryClient, model) which wraps the Responses API.
   772→    Returns (None, None) when no provider is available.
   773→    """
   774→    forced = _get_auxiliary_provider(task)
   775→    if forced != "auto":
   776→        return resolve_provider_client(forced, async_mode=True)
   777→    return resolve_provider_client("auto", async_mode=True)
   778→
   779→
   780→def get_vision_auxiliary_client() -> Tuple[Optional[OpenAI], Optional[str]]:
   781→    """Return (client, default_model_slug) for vision/multimodal auxiliary tasks.
   782→
   783→    Checks AUXILIARY_VISION_PROVIDER for a forced provider, otherwise
   784→    auto-detects.  Callers may override the returned model with
   785→    AUXILIARY_VISION_MODEL.
   786→
   787→    In auto mode, only providers known to support multimodal are tried:
   788→    OpenRouter, Nous Portal, and Codex OAuth (gpt-5.3-codex supports
   789→    vision via the Responses API).  Custom endpoints and API-key
   790→    providers are skipped — they may not handle vision input.  To use
   791→    them, set AUXILIARY_VISION_PROVIDER explicitly.
   792→    """
   793→    forced = _get_auxiliary_provider("vision")
   794→    if forced != "auto":
   795→        return resolve_provider_client(forced)
   796→    # Auto: try providers known to support multimodal first, then fall
   797→    # back to the user's custom endpoint.  Many local models (Qwen-VL,
   798→    # LLaVA, Pixtral, etc.) support vision — skipping them entirely
   799→    # caused silent failures for local-only users.
   800→    for try_fn in (_try_openrouter, _try_nous, _try_codex,
   801→                   _try_custom_endpoint):
   802→        client, model = try_fn()
   803→        if client is not None:
   804→            return client, model
   805→    logger.debug("Auxiliary vision client: none available")
   806→    return None, None
   807→
   808→
   809→def get_async_vision_auxiliary_client():
   810→    """Return (async_client, model_slug) for async vision consumers.
   811→
   812→    Properly handles Codex routing — unlike manually constructing
   813→    AsyncOpenAI from a sync client, this preserves the Responses API
   814→    adapter for Codex providers.

"""
GLM 4.7 tool call parser.

Same as GLM 4.5 but with slightly different regex patterns.
The tool_call tags may wrap differently and arg parsing handles
newlines between key/value pairs.

Based on VLLM's Glm47MoeModelToolParser (extends Glm4MoeModelToolParser).
"""

import re

from environments.tool_call_parsers import register_parser
from environments.tool_call_parsers.glm45_parser import Glm45ToolCallParser


@register_parser("glm47")
class Glm47ToolCallParser(Glm45ToolCallParser):
    """
    Parser for GLM 4.7 tool calls.
    Extends GLM 4.5 with updated regex patterns.
    """

    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        # GLM 4.7 uses a slightly different detail regex that includes
        # the <tool_call> wrapper and optional arg_key content
        self.FUNC_DETAIL_REGEX = re.compile(
            r"<tool_call>(.*?)(<arg_key>.*?)?</tool_call>", re.DOTALL
        )
        # GLM 4.7 handles newlines between arg_key and arg_value tags
        self.FUNC_ARG_REGEX = re.compile(
            r"<arg_key>(.*?)</arg_key>(?:\\n|\s)*<arg_value>(.*?)</arg_value>",
            re.DOTALL,
        )
```

860→# Every auxiliary LLM consumer should use these instead of manually
   861→# constructing clients and calling .chat.completions.create().
   862→
   863→# Client cache: (provider, async_mode) -> (client, default_model)
   864→# Protected by _client_cache_lock for thread safety — subagent threads
   865→# call call_llm() concurrently for compression, session search, etc.
   866→import threading
   867→_client_cache: Dict[tuple, tuple] = {}
   868→_client_cache_lock = threading.Lock()
   869→
   870→
   871→def _get_cached_client(
   872→    provider: str, model: str = None, async_mode: bool = False,
   873→) -> Tuple[Optional[Any], Optional[str]]:
   874→    """Get or create a cached client for the given provider.
   875→
   876→    Thread-safe: multiple subagent threads may resolve clients concurrently.
   877→    """
   878→    cache_key = (provider, async_mode)
   879→    with _client_cache_lock:
   880→        if cache_key in _client_cache:
   881→            cached_client, cached_default = _client_cache[cache_key]
   882→            return cached_client, model or cached_default
   883→    # Build outside the lock — resolve_provider_client can be slow (network)
   884→    client, default_model = resolve_provider_client(provider, model, async_mode)
   885→    if client is not None:
   886→        with _client_cache_lock:
   887→            # Double-check: another thread may have populated it
   888→            if cache_key not in _client_cache:
   889→                _client_cache[cache_key] = (client, default_model)
   890→            else:
   891→                # Use the already-cached client to avoid duplicates
   892→                client, default_model = _client_cache[cache_key]
   893→    return client, model or default_model
   894→

   897→    task: str = None,
   898→    provider: str = None,
   899→    model: str = None,
   900→) -> Tuple[str, Optional[str]]:
   901→    """Determine provider + model for a call.
   902→
   903→    Priority:
   904→      1. Explicit provider/model args (always win)
   905→      2. Env var overrides (AUXILIARY_{TASK}_PROVIDER, etc.)
   906→      3. Config file (auxiliary.{task}.provider/model or compression.*)
   907→      4. "auto" (full auto-detection chain)
   908→
   909→    Returns (provider, model) where model may be None (use provider default).

   554→
   555→def _resolve_task_credentials(task: dict, default_creds: dict, parent_agent) -> dict:
   556→    """Resolve per-task credentials, falling back to config-level defaults.
   557→
   558→    If the task specifies a provider (and optionally model), resolve fresh
   559→    credentials via the shared :func:`resolve_provider_credentials` helper.
   560→    Otherwise use the default_creds from the delegation config.
   561→    """
   562→    task_provider = (task.get("provider") or "").strip().lower()
   563→    task_model = (task.get("model") or "").strip()
   564→
   565→    if not task_provider:
   566→        # No per-task provider — use config defaults, but allow model override
   567→        result = dict(default_creds)
   568→        if task_model:
   569→            result["model"] = task_model
   570→        return result
   571→
   572→    # Per-task provider specified — resolve credentials

  3219→        tools. Used by the concurrent execution path; the sequential path retains
  3220→        its own inline invocation for backward-compatible display handling.

  3222→        if function_name == "todo":

  3239→            )
  3240→        elif function_name == "memory":
  3241→            target = function_args.get("target", "memory")

  3243→            result = _memory_tool(

  3246→                content=function_args.get("content"),
  3247→                old_text=function_args.get("old_text"),
  3248→                store=self._memory_store,
  3249→            )
  3250→            # Also send user observations to Honcho when active
  3251→            if self._honcho and target == "user" and function_args.get("action") == "add":
  3252→                self._honcho_save_user_observation(function_args.get("content", ""))

  3254→        elif function_name == "clarify":
  3255→            from tools.clarify_tool import clarify_tool as _clarify_tool

  3261→        elif function_name == "delegate_task":

  2585→    # ── Provider credential swap ─────────────────────────────────────────
  2586→
  2587→    def _apply_provider_credentials(self, creds: dict) -> None:
  2588→        """Apply a resolved credentials bundle to this agent in-place.
  2589→
  2590→        *creds* is a dict from :func:`resolve_provider_credentials` with
  2591→        keys: provider, model, base_url, api_key, api_mode, client.
  2592→
  2593→        Handles the OpenAI-vs-Anthropic client split and re-evaluates
  2594→        prompt-caching eligibility.
  2595→        """
  2596→        self.model = creds["model"]
  2597→        self.provider = creds["provider"]
  2598→        self.base_url = creds["base_url"]
  2599→        self.api_mode = creds["api_mode"]
  2600→
  2601→        if creds["api_mode"] == "anthropic_messages":
  2602→            from agent.anthropic_adapter import build_anthropic_client, resolve_anthropic_token
  2603→            effective_key = creds["api_key"] or resolve_anthropic_token() or ""
  2604→            self._anthropic_api_key = effective_key
  2605→            self._anthropic_client = build_anthropic_client(effective_key)
  2606→            self.client = None
  2607→            self._client_kwargs = {}
  2608→        else:
  2609→            self.client = creds["client"]
  2610→            self._client_kwargs = {
  2611→                "api_key": creds["api_key"],
  2612→                "base_url": creds["base_url"],
  2613→            }
  2614→
  2615→        # Re-evaluate prompt caching for the new provider/model
  2616→        is_native_anthropic = creds["api_mode"] == "anthropic_messages"
  2617→        self._use_prompt_caching = (
  2618→            ("openrouter" in creds["base_url"].lower()
  2619→             and "claude" in creds["model"].lower())
  2620→            or is_native_anthropic
  2621→        )
  2622→
  2623→    # ── Provider fallback ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
  2624→
  2625→    def _try_activate_fallback(self) -> bool:
  2626→        """Switch to the configured fallback model/provider.
  2627→
  2628→        Called when the primary model is failing after retries.  Swaps the
  2629→        OpenAI client, model slug, and provider in-place so the retry loop
  2630→        can continue with the new backend.  One-shot: returns False if
  2631→        already activated or not configured.
  2632→
  2633→        Uses the centralized provider router (resolve_provider_client) for
  2634→        auth resolution and client construction — no duplicated provider→key
  2635→        mappings.
  2636→        """
  2637→        if self._fallback_activated or not self._fallback_model:
  2638→            return False
  2639→
  2640→        fb = self._fallback_model
  2641→        fb_provider = (fb.get("provider") or "").strip().lower()
  2642→        fb_model = (fb.get("model") or "").strip()
  2643→        if not fb_provider or not fb_model:
  2644→            return False
  2645→
  2646→        try:
  2647→            from agent.auxiliary_client import resolve_provider_credentials
  2648→            creds = resolve_provider_credentials(fb_provider, fb_model)
  2649→            if creds is None:
  2650→                logging.warning(
  2651→                    "Fallback to %s failed: provider not configured",
  2652→                    fb_provider)
  2653→                return False
  2654→
  2655→            old_model = self.model
  2656→            self._apply_provider_credentials(creds)
  2657→            self._fallback_activated = True
  2658→
  2659→            print(
  2660→                f"{self.log_prefix}🔄 Primary model failed — switching to fallback: "
  2661→                f"{fb_model} via {fb_provider}"
  2662→            )
  2663→            logging.info(
  2664→                "Fallback activated: %s → %s (%s)",
  2665→                old_model, fb_model, fb_provider,
  2666→            )
  2667→            return True
  2668→        except Exception as e:
  2669→            logging.error("Failed to activate fallback model: %s", e)
  2670→            return False
  2671→
  2672→    # ── Explicit model switch (switch_model tool) ───────────────────────────
  2673→
  2674→    def _switch_model(self, provider: str, model: str) -> dict:
  2675→        """Switch the active model/provider in-place.
  2676→
  2677→        Called by the switch_model tool.  Returns a result dict
  2678→        (success/failure + current state).
  2679→        """
  2680→        provider = provider.strip().lower()
  2681→        model = model.strip()

   100→[{dim}]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠈⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]"""
   101→
   102→
   103→def _build_compact_banner() -> str:
   104→    """Build the compact banner using active skin colors."""
   105→    title = _skin_color("banner_title", "#FFD700")
   106→    accent = _skin_color("banner_accent", "#FFBF00")
   107→    border = _skin_color("banner_border", "#CD7F32")
   108→    dim = _skin_color("banner_dim", "#B8860B")
   109→    return f"""
   110→[bold {title}]╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗[/]
   111→[bold {title}]║[/]  [{accent}]⚕ NOUS HERMES[/] [dim {dim}]- AI Agent Framework[/]              [bold {title}]║[/]
   112→[bold {title}]║[/]  [{border}]Messenger of the Digital Gods[/]    [dim {dim}]Nous Research[/]   [bold {title}]║[/]
   113→[bold {title}]╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝[/]
   114→"""
   115→
   116→
   117→# Backward-compatible aliases (now dynamic)
   118→HERMES_AGENT_LOGO = None  # Use _build_hermes_logo() instead
   119→HERMES_CADUCEUS = None    # Use _build_hermes_caduceus() instead
   120→COMPACT_BANNER = None     # Use _build_compact_banner() instead
   121→
   122→
   123→# =========================================================================
   124→# Skills scanning
   125→# =========================================================================
   126→
   127→def get_available_skills() -> Dict[str, List[str]]:
   128→    """Scan ~/.hermes/skills/ and return skills grouped by category."""
   129→    import os
   130→
   131→    hermes_home = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes"))
   132→    skills_dir = hermes_home / "skills"
   133→    skills_by_category = {}
   134→
   135→    if not skills_dir.exists():
   136→        return skills_by_category
   137→
   138→    for skill_file in skills_dir.rglob("SKILL.md"):
   139→        rel_path = skill_file.relative_to(skills_dir)
   140→        parts = rel_path.parts
   141→        if len(parts) >= 2:
   142→            category = parts[0]
   143→            skill_name = parts[-2]
   144→        else:
   145→            category = "general"
   146→            skill_name = skill_file.parent.name
   147→        skills_by_category.setdefault(category, []).append(skill_name)
   148→
   149→    return skills_by_category
   150→
   151→
   152→# =========================================================================
   153→# Update check
   154→# =========================================================================
   155→
   156→# Cache update check results for 6 hours to avoid repeated git fetches
   157→_UPDATE_CHECK_CACHE_SECONDS = 6 * 3600
   158→
   159→
   160→def check_for_updates() -> Optional[int]:
   161→    """Check how many commits behind origin/main the local repo is.
   162→
   163→    Does a ``git fetch`` at most once every 6 hours (cached to
   164→    ``~/.hermes/.update_check``).  Returns the number of commits behind,
   165→    or ``None`` if the check fails or isn't applicable.
   166→    """
   167→    hermes_home = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes"))
   168→    repo_dir = hermes_home / "hermes-agent"
   169→    cache_file = hermes_home / ".update_check"
   170→
   171→    # Must be a git repo
   172→    if not (repo_dir / ".git").exists():
   173→        return None
   174→
   175→    # Read cache
   176→    now = time.time()
   177→    try:
   178→        if cache_file.exists():
   179→            cached = json.loads(cache_file.read_text())
   180→            if now - cached.get("ts", 0) < _UPDATE_CHECK_CACHE_SECONDS:
   181→                return cached.get("behind")
   182→    except Exception:
   183→        pass
   184→
   185→    # Fetch latest refs (fast — only downloads ref metadata, no files)
   186→    try:
   187→        subprocess.run(
   188→            ["git", "fetch", "origin", "--quiet"],
   189→            capture_output=True, timeout=10,
   190→            cwd=str(repo_dir),
   191→        )
   192→    except Exception:
   193→        pass  # Offline or timeout — use stale refs, that's fine
   194→
   195→    # Count commits behind
   196→    try:
   197→        result = subprocess.run(
   198→            ["git", "rev-list", "--count", "HEAD..origin/main"],
   199→            capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5,
   200→            cwd=str(repo_dir),
   201→        )
   202→        if result.returncode == 0:
   203→            behind = int(result.stdout.strip())
   204→        else:
   205→            behind = None
   206→    except Exception:
   207→        behind = None
   208→
   209→    # Write cache
   210→    try:
   211→        cache_file.write_text(json.dumps({"ts": now, "behind": behind}))
   212→    except Exception:
   213→        pass
   214→
   215→    return behind
   216→
   217→
   218→# =========================================================================
   219→# Welcome banner
   220→# =========================================================================
   221→
   222→def _format_context_length(tokens: int) -> str:
   223→    """Format a token count for display (e.g. 128000 → '128K', 1048576 → '1M')."""
   224→    if tokens >= 1_000_000:
   225→        val = tokens / 1_000_000
   226→        return f"{val:g}M"
   227→    elif tokens >= 1_000:
   228→        val = tokens / 1_000
   229→        return f"{val:g}K"
   230→    return str(tokens)
   231→
   232→
   233→def build_welcome_banner(console: Console, model: str, cwd: str,
   234→                         tools: List[dict] = None,
   235→                         enabled_toolsets: List[str] = None,
   236→                         session_id: str = None,
   237→                         get_toolset_for_tool=None,
   238→                         context_length: int = None):
   239→    """Build and print a welcome banner with caduceus on left and info on right.
   240→
   241→    Args:
   242→        console: Rich Console instance.
   243→        model: Current model name.
   244→        cwd: Current working directory.
   245→        tools: List of tool definitions.
   246→        enabled_toolsets: List of enabled toolset names.
   247→        session_id: Session identifier.
   248→        get_toolset_for_tool: Callable to map tool name -> toolset name.
   249→        context_length: Model's context window size in tokens.
   250→    """
   251→    from model_tools import check_tool_availability
   252→    if get_toolset_for_tool is None:
   253→        from model_tools import get_toolset_for_tool
   254→
   255→    tools = tools or []
   256→    enabled_toolsets = enabled_toolsets or []
   257→
   258→    _, unavailable_toolsets = check_tool_availability(quiet=True)
   259→    disabled_tools = set()
   260→    for item in unavailable_toolsets:
   261→        disabled_tools.update(item.get("tools", []))
   262→
   263→    layout_table = Table.grid(padding=(0, 2))
   264→    layout_table.add_column("left", justify="center")
   265→    layout_table.add_column("right", justify="left")
   266→
   267→    # Resolve skin colors once for the entire banner
   268→    accent = _skin_color("banner_accent", "#FFBF00")
   269→    dim = _skin_color("banner_dim", "#B8860B")
   270→    text = _skin_color("banner_text", "#FFF8DC")
   271→    session_color = _skin_color("session_border", "#8B8682")
   272→
   273→
   274→    # Use skin's banner_hero if available, else dynamic caduceus
   275→    try:
   276→        from hermes_cli.skin_engine import get_active_skin
   277→        _hero = get_active_skin().banner_hero
   278→    except Exception:
   279→        _hero = ""
   280→    left_lines = ["", _hero or _build_hermes_caduceus(), ""]
   281→    model_short = model.split("/")[-1] if "/" in model else model
   282→    if len(model_short) > 28:
   283→        model_short = model_short[:25] + "..."
   284→    ctx_str = f" [dim {dim}]·[/] [dim {dim}]{_format_context_length(context_length)} context[/]" if context_length else ""
   285→    left_lines.append(f"[{accent}]{model_short}[/]{ctx_str} [dim {dim}]·[/] [dim {dim}]Nous Research[/]")
   286→    left_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}]{cwd}[/]")
   287→    if session_id:
   288→        left_lines.append(f"[dim {session_color}]Session: {session_id}[/]")
   289→    left_content = "\n".join(left_lines)
   290→
   291→    right_lines = [f"[bold {accent}]Available Tools[/]"]
   292→    toolsets_dict: Dict[str, list] = {}
   293→
   294→    for tool in tools:
   295→        tool_name = tool["function"]["name"]
   296→        toolset = get_toolset_for_tool(tool_name) or "other"
   297→        toolsets_dict.setdefault(toolset, []).append(tool_name)
   298→
   299→    for item in unavailable_toolsets:
   300→        toolset_id = item.get("id", item.get("name", "unknown"))
   301→        display_name = f"{toolset_id}_tools" if not toolset_id.endswith("_tools") else toolset_id
   302→        if display_name not in toolsets_dict:
   303→            toolsets_dict[display_name] = []
   304→        for tool_name in item.get("tools", []):
   305→            if tool_name not in toolsets_dict[display_name]:
   306→                toolsets_dict[display_name].append(tool_name)
   307→
   308→    sorted_toolsets = sorted(toolsets_dict.keys())
   309→    display_toolsets = sorted_toolsets[:8]
   310→    remaining_toolsets = len(sorted_toolsets) - 8
   311→
   312→    for toolset in display_toolsets:
   313→        tool_names = toolsets_dict[toolset]
   314→        colored_names = []
   315→        for name in sorted(tool_names):
   316→            if name in disabled_tools:
   317→                colored_names.append(f"[red]{name}[/]")
   318→            else:
   319→                colored_names.append(f"[{text}]{name}[/]")
   320→
   321→        tools_str = ", ".join(colored_names)
   322→        if len(", ".join(sorted(tool_names))) > 45:
   323→            short_names = []
   324→            length = 0
   325→            for name in sorted(tool_names):
   326→                if length + len(name) + 2 > 42:
   327→                    short_names.append("...")
   328→                    break
   329→                short_names.append(name)
   330→                length += len(name) + 2
   331→            colored_names = []
   332→            for name in short_names:
   333→                if name == "...":
   334→                    colored_names.append("[dim]...[/]")
   335→                elif name in disabled_tools:
   336→                    colored_names.append(f"[red]{name}[/]")
   337→                else:
   338→                    colored_names.append(f"[{text}]{name}[/]")
   339→            tools_str = ", ".join(colored_names)
   340→
   341→        right_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}]{toolset}:[/] {tools_str}")
   342→
   343→    if remaining_toolsets > 0:
   344→        right_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}](and {remaining_toolsets} more toolsets...)[/]")
   345→
   346→    # MCP Servers section (only if configured)
   347→    try:
   348→        from tools.mcp_tool import get_mcp_status
   349→        mcp_status = get_mcp_status()
   350→    except Exception:
   351→        mcp_status = []
   352→
   353→    if mcp_status:
   354→        right_lines.append("")
   355→        right_lines.append(f"[bold {accent}]MCP Servers[/]")
   356→        for srv in mcp_status:
   357→            if srv["connected"]:
   358→                right_lines.append(
   359→                    f"[dim {dim}]{srv['name']}[/] [{text}]({srv['transport']})[/] "
   360→                    f"[dim {dim}]—[/] [{text}]{srv['tools']} tool(s)[/]"
   361→                )
   362→            else:
   363→                right_lines.append(
   364→                    f"[red]{srv['name']}[/] [dim]({srv['transport']})[/] "
   365→                    f"[red]— failed[/]"
   366→                )
   367→
   368→    right_lines.append("")
   369→    right_lines.append(f"[bold {accent}]Available Skills[/]")
   370→    skills_by_category = get_available_skills()
   371→    total_skills = sum(len(s) for s in skills_by_category.values())
   372→
   373→    if skills_by_category:
   374→        for category in sorted(skills_by_category.keys()):
   375→            skill_names = sorted(skills_by_category[category])
   376→            if len(skill_names) > 8:
   377→                display_names = skill_names[:8]
   378→                skills_str = ", ".join(display_names) + f" +{len(skill_names) - 8} more"
   379→            else:
   380→                skills_str = ", ".join(skill_names)
   381→            if len(skills_str) > 50:
   382→                skills_str = skills_str[:47] + "..."
   383→            right_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}]{category}:[/] [{text}]{skills_str}[/]")
   384→    else:
   385→        right_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}]No skills installed[/]")
   386→
   387→    right_lines.append("")
   388→    mcp_connected = sum(1 for s in mcp_status if s["connected"]) if mcp_status else 0
   389→    summary_parts = [f"{len(tools)} tools", f"{total_skills} skills"]
   390→    if mcp_connected:
   391→        summary_parts.append(f"{mcp_connected} MCP servers")
   392→    summary_parts.append("/help for commands")
   393→    right_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}]{' · '.join(summary_parts)}[/]")
   394→
   395→    # Update check — show if behind origin/main
   396→    try:
   397→        behind = check_for_updates()
   398→        if behind and behind > 0:
   399→            commits_word = "commit" if behind == 1 else "commits"
   400→            right_lines.append(
   401→                f"[bold yellow]⚠ {behind} {commits_word} behind[/]"
   402→                f"[dim yellow] — run [bold]hermes update[/bold] to update[/]"
   403→            )
   404→    except Exception:
   405→        pass  # Never break the banner over an update check
   406→
   407→    right_content = "\n".join(right_lines)
   408→    layout_table.add_row(left_content, right_content)
   409→
   410→    agent_name = _skin_branding("agent_name", "Hermes Agent")
   411→    title_color = _skin_color("banner_title", "#FFD700")
   412→    border_color = _skin_color("banner_border", "#CD7F32")
   413→    outer_panel = Panel(
   414→        layout_table,
   415→        title=f"[bold {title_color}]{agent_name} v{VERSION} ({RELEASE_DATE})[/]",
   416→        border_style=border_color,
   417→        padding=(0, 2),
   418→    )
   419→
   420→    # Use skin's banner_logo if available, else dynamic logo
   421→    try:
   422→        from hermes_cli.skin_engine import get_active_skin
   423→        _logo = get_active_skin().banner_logo
   424→    except Exception:
   425→        _logo = ""
   426→
   427→    console.print()
   428→    console.print(_logo or _build_hermes_logo())
   429→    console.print()
   430→    console.print(outer_panel)
   431→
```

tool_result Bash
```
### Review workflow

Invoke the reviewer agent for each group of dimensions sequentially.
Even without parallelism, isolating dimensions across separate agent
invocations prevents score bleed between concerns.

Merge assessments and findings, then import.

When Gemini CLI adds parallel subagent execution, split dimensions across
concurrent agent calls instead.

<!-- desloppify-overlay: gemini -->
<!-- desloppify-end -->

## Hermes Agent Overlay

Hermes has built-in parallel subagent support via `delegate_task` (up to 3
concurrent children). Use batch mode for review dimensions.

### Review workflow

1. **Prepare prompts:**
```bash
desloppify review --run-batches --dry-run
```
This generates per-batch prompt files in `.desloppify/subagents/runs/<run-id>/prompts/`
and prints the run directory path. Note the run directory — you need it for every step.

2. **Launch subagents in groups of 3** using `delegate_task` with the `tasks` array.
Each subagent reads its prompt file, inspects the repository, and writes its
result. Subagents must not edit repository source files — only write the result.

Output filename is `batch-N.raw.txt` (not `.json`) — the importer requires this exact name.

Example `delegate_task` call:
```
delegate_task(tasks=[
  {
    "goal": "Review batch 1. Read the prompt at .desloppify/subagents/runs/<run-id>/prompts/batch-1.md, follow it exactly, inspect the repository, and write ONLY valid JSON to .desloppify/subagents/runs/<run-id>/results/batch-1.raw.txt.",
    "context": "Repository root: <cwd>. Blind packet: .desloppify/review_packet_blind.json. The prompt file defines the required output schema. Do not edit repository source files. Only write the review result file.",
```

        "keep_alive": False,
        "custom_timeout": False,

    session_config = {
        "projectId": config["project_id"],
    }

## Detailed Code Review: Commit 066fb13 - Thread Safety Analysis

Based on my thorough analysis of the commit, here are my critical findings:

### 1. **hermes_state.py - Threading.Lock Usage: CRITICAL ISSUE FOUND**

**Status:** Lock is properly used in most methods, BUT there is a **data race in `_init_schema()`**.

**Issue:**
- **File:Line:** `hermes_state.py:120-174`
- `_init_schema()` is called from `__init__` (line 118) WITHOUT holding `self._lock`
- Schema initialization happens BEFORE the lock is initialized and can race with concurrent queries
- Even though SQLite has thread-safe connection mode (`check_same_thread=False`), concurrent ALTER TABLE and PRAGMA statements can cause corruption

**Deadlock Risk:** 
- **NONE FOUND** - The lock usage is single-threaded per method with no nested locks and straightforward acquire/release

**Missing Locks:**
- `_init_schema()` should either be called with the lock held OR the lock should be initialized before `_init_schema()` is called
- All other public methods (create_session, end_session, get_messages, etc.) are properly protected

**Recommendation:**
Initialize the lock BEFORE calling `_init_schema()` in `__init__()`:
```python
self._lock = threading.Lock()  # Move to line 108, before _init_schema()
```

---

### 2. **auxiliary_client.py - Client Cache Lock: WELL-DESIGNED**

**Status:** CORRECT implementation with proper double-check locking pattern

**Key Design Strengths:**
- **File:Line:** `agent/auxiliary_client.py:866-893`
- Lock placed at module level to protect shared `_client_cache` dictionary
- Lock is only held during cache lookups and updates (line 879-882, 886-892)
- Expensive `resolve_provider_client()` call (line 884) happens OUTSIDE the lock
- Proper double-check pattern: checks cache again after resolving (lines 887-892) to avoid duplicate clients
- Thread-safe: subagent threads calling `call_llm()` concurrently will correctly share cached clients

**Minor Over-Engineering:**
- `resolve_provider_credentials()` (lines 705-746) is a thin wrapper that calls `resolve_provider_client()` and adds api_mode derivation
- This design is acceptable - the function is well-documented and handles the model switching use cases

**No Issues:** This is sound code.

---

### 3. **delegate_tool.py - Child Agent Registration: CORRECT WITH GOOD DESIGN**

**Status:** Properly refactored with correct lock usage

**Key Changes:**
- **File:Line:** `tools/delegate_tool.py:159-241` (_build_child_agent) and **244-379** (_run_single_child refactored)
- **Split correctly:** SSL/httpx client construction is NOT thread-safe, so moving it to main thread (line 204-229) is correct
- **Lock usage in registration:** Lines 234-239 correctly protect list append with lock
- **Lock usage in unregistration:** Lines 372-379 correctly protect list remove with lock

**Registration Logic (lines 233-240):**
```python
if hasattr(parent_agent, '_active_children'):
    lock = getattr(parent_agent, '_active_children_lock', None)
    if lock:
        with lock:
            parent_agent._active_children.append(child)
    else:
        parent_agent._active_children.append(child)
```

**Issue Found: INCOMPLETE LOCK FALLBACK**
- If `_active_children_lock` doesn't exist, it appends without a lock (line 239)
- This can race with `interrupt()` which iterates over `_active_children` (run_agent.py:1397-1398)
- Should either guarantee lock exists or raise an error

**Recommendation:**
Remove the fallback case since AIAgent should always have `_active_children_lock`:
```python
if hasattr(parent_agent, '_active_children'):
    with parent_agent._active_children_lock:
        parent_agent._active_children.append(child)
```

---

### 4. **run_agent.py - New Fields and Their Usage: ISSUES FOUND**

**4a. `_original_model` Field (lines 334-335)**

**Status:** Properly initialized and used

**Implementation:**
```python
self._original_model = {"provider": self.provider, "model": self.model}
```
- Initialized in `__init__` (line 334)
- Safely read in two places: `_build_api_kwargs()` (line 1709) and switch_model tool (line 3280)
- No mutations after initialization
- **No thread safety issues**

---

**4b. `_pending_model_switch` Field (lines 348, 4121-4124, 4149-4152)**

**Status:** CRITICAL RACE CONDITION FOUND**

**Issue:**
- **File:Line:** `run_agent.py:348` (initialization), **4121-4124** and **4149-4152** (reads/clears)
- This field is meant to be set by external threads (control API) but consumed by main agent thread
- **No synchronization:** The field is NOT protected by a lock
- **Race condition scenario:**
  1. External thread sets `_pending_model_switch = {"provider": "openrouter", "model": "claude"}`
  2. Main thread checks `if self._pending_model_switch:` (line 4121)
  3. External thread sets it to a different value or None
  4. Main thread reads inconsistent state at line 4122

**Proof of Race:**
```python
# Thread 1 (external control):
self.agent._pending_model_switch = {"provider": "openrouter", "model": "gpt4"}

# Thread 2 (main agent loop):
if self._pending_model_switch:           # Line 4121 - TRUE
    _ps = self._pending_model_switch     # Line 4122 - might be None or different now!
    self._pending_model_switch = None    # Line 4123
```

**Recommendation:**
Add a lock for `_pending_model_switch`:
```python
self._pending_model_switch_lock = threading.Lock()

# To set (from control API):
with self._pending_model_switch_lock:
    self._pending_model_switch = creds

# To consume (in run_conversation):
with self._pending_model_switch_lock:
    if self._pending_model_switch:
        _ps = self._pending_model_switch
        self._pending_model_switch = None
```

---

**4c. `_active_children_lock` Field (line 353)**

**Status:** Properly initialized and used with MINOR inconsistency

**Implementation:**
```python
self._active_children_lock = threading.Lock()  # Line 353
```

**Usage:**
- Correctly used in `interrupt()` method (lines 1397-1398):
  ```python
  with self._active_children_lock:
      children_snapshot = list(self._active_children)
  ```
- Correctly used in delegate_tool registration/unregistration (checked with `getattr`)

**Minor Issue:**
- The getattr fallback in delegate_tool.py (lines 234-239, 371-377) suggests defensive coding, but should be replaced with direct access since we control AIAgent

---

### 5. **Thread Safety Issues Summary**

**CRITICAL Issues:**
1. `hermes_state.py:_init_schema()` - Race condition on schema initialization (unfixed)
2. `run_agent.py:_pending_model_switch` - No synchronization for external model switch requests (unfixed)

**MODERATE Issues:**
3. `delegate_tool.py` - Fallback lock-free code path on append/remove (defensive but risky)

**GOOD Design:**
4. `auxiliary_client.py:_client_cache_lock` - Double-check locking pattern is correct
5. `run_agent.py:_active_children_lock` - Proper use for interrupt propagation
6. `hermes_state.py` - All database operations properly locked after initialization

---

### Critical File:Line References

| File | Line(s) | Issue | Severity |
|------|---------|-------|----------|
| hermes_state.py | 104-118 | Lock initialized after `_init_schema()` called | CRITICAL |
| run_agent.py | 348, 4121-4124, 4149-4152 | `_pending_model_switch` has data race | CRITICAL |
| tools/delegate_tool.py | 234-239, 371-379 | Fallback code path removes lock protection | MODERATE |
| agent/auxiliary_client.py | 866-893 | Double-check locking (CORRECT) | — |
| run_agent.py | 353, 1397-1398 | `_active_children_lock` usage (CORRECT) | — |

## Critical Review of Commits 9309c4f and 8058d57

### **CRITICAL ISSUE 1: _run_cleanup() Control Flow Bug - cli.py:449, 453, 458**

**Severity: HIGH - Functions silently fail**

The refactoring changed exception handlers from `pass` to `return None`. This introduces a **control flow bug**:

```python
def _run_cleanup():
    """Run resource cleanup exactly once."""
    global _cleanup_done
    if _cleanup_done:
        return
    _cleanup_done = True
    try:
        _cleanup_all_terminals()
    except Exception:
        return None  # BUG: Returns early, skips remaining cleanup
    try:
        _cleanup_all_browsers()
    except Exception:
        return None  # BUG: Returns early, skips MCP shutdown
    try:
        from tools.mcp_tool import shutdown_mcp_servers
        shutdown_mcp_servers()
    except Exception:
        return None
```

If `_cleanup_all_terminals()` fails (line 449), the function returns immediately without running `_cleanup_all_browsers()` or `shutdown_mcp_servers()`. The original `pass` statements allowed execution to continue to the next try block. **This is a regression that breaks cleanup logic.**

**Fix:** Use `pass` or restructure with `continue` in a loop/context, or consolidate all exceptions and log them.

---

### **CRITICAL ISSUE 2: AGENTS.md Duplicate Agent Blocks - AGENTS.md:350-381**

**Severity: HIGH - Malformed config**

Three **identical** `desloppify` agent config blocks appear consecutively (lines 350-359, 361-370, 372-381) with no content between them:

```markdown
---
name: desloppify
description: >
  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker...
allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
---

---
name: desloppify
description: >
  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker...
allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
---

---
name: desloppify
description: >
  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker...
allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
---
```

This violates agent config syntax. Parser behavior is undefined — it may register only the first, the last, or all three (causing runtime errors). The commit message claims this "adds desloppify agent config" but includes **3 copies** with **no explanation** for the duplication.

---

### **MODERATE ISSUE 3: Removed Imports Used Elsewhere - cli.py:424, 426**

**Severity: MODERATE - Missing unused imports may mask real issues**

The commit removed from cli.py (lines 424, 426 in original):
- `from hermes_cli.commands import COMMANDS` (removed, not used)
- `from hermes_cli import callbacks as _callbacks` (removed, not used)
- `from toolsets import resolve_toolset` (removed from cli.py)

Grep confirms `COMMANDS` is not used in cli.py, but the module name is suspicious. The removal of `_callbacks` requires verification that callback registration isn't silently broken elsewhere.

**Low risk** per grep output (no references found), but the pattern suggests incomplete refactoring.

---

### **MODERATE ISSUE 4: tools_config.py Dead Code Removal - Unverified**

**Severity: LOW-MODERATE - Possibly safe, but not audited**

The commit removed 196 lines of "large unused configurator UI block" including:
- `_prompt_yes_no()` function (lines 57-68)
- `CONFIGURABLE_TOOLSETS`, `_DEFAULT_OFF_TOOLSETS`, `PLATFORMS`, `TOOL_CATEGORIES`, `TOOLSET_ENV_REQUIREMENTS` dicts
- Post-setup hooks infrastructure

**The commit message claims this is "dead code"**, but:
1. No evidence provided that these dicts aren't referenced in `_configure_tool_category()` or `_configure_provider()` (which remain in the file)
2. These look like **intentionally disabled UI code**, not dead code
3. **No audit trail** showing grep/rg results confirming zero references

**Grep verification** of the remaining code shows references to `cat.get("setup_title")`, `provider.get("name")`, etc., which suggests the removed dicts may have been the **source** of that data in previous code paths.

---

### **MINOR ISSUE 5: Misaligned Import Removal in hermes_cli/main.py - main.py:727**

**Severity: LOW - Deferred import catches it**

The commit removed `save_config` from line 727's imports in `cmd_model()`:
```python
-    from hermes_cli.config import load_config, save_config, get_env_value, save_env_value
+    from hermes_cli.config import load_config, get_env_value
```

However, `save_config` **is still used at line 940** (inside a nested function). The removal doesn't break because:
- Line 932 re-imports it locally: `from hermes_cli.config import load_config, save_config`
- But this is **fragile** and **sloppy** — imports should be at function scope, not scattered

**Better pattern:** Keep the import at function scope consistently.

---

### **MINOR ISSUE 6: shutil.which() "Improvement" - cli.py:475, 525, 591, 607, 616, 657, 668, 674, 679**

**Severity: LOW - Minimal practical value**

The commit changed:
```python
-            ["git", "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
+            [shutil.which("git") or "git", "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
```

Repeated 9 times across `_git_repo_root()`, `_setup_worktree()`, `_check_worktree_active()`, and `_cleanup_worktree()`.

**Analysis:**
- `shutil.which("git")` returns `None` if git is not in PATH
- Fallback `or "git"` is used when `which()` fails
- **Problem:** If git is not in PATH, subprocess will still fail with the bare `"git"` command
- **Improvement is nominal:** In practice, if git isn't in PATH, the code fails either way
- **No actual benefit:** The try/except already handles subprocess failures gracefully

This is **noise masquerading as improvement** — it doesn't strengthen error handling.

---

### **Summary Table**

| Issue | File | Line(s) | Severity | Type | Action Required |
|-------|------|---------|----------|------|-----------------|
| 1 | cli.py | 449, 453, 458 | **CRITICAL** | Logic bug | Revert `return None` to `pass` |
| 2 | AGENTS.md | 350-381 | **CRITICAL** | Malformed config | Remove 2 of 3 duplicate blocks |
| 3 | cli.py | 424, 426 | MODERATE | Removed imports | Verify `_callbacks` not used elsewhere |
| 4 | tools_config.py | (196 lines) | MODERATE | Unverified dead code removal | Audit against actual usage or restore |
| 5 | hermes_cli/main.py | 727, 932 | LOW | Fragmented imports | Consolidate imports at function scope |
| 6 | cli.py | 475, 525, 591+ | LOW | Cosmetic "improvement" | Safe to keep, but adds no value |

---

### **Recommendations**

1. **Immediately revert** commit 9309c4f changes to `_run_cleanup()` (lines 449, 453, 458)
2. **Fix** commit 8058d57 by removing duplicate desloppify blocks from AGENTS.md
3. **Audit** tools_config.py removal by running: `git log -p --all -S 'CONFIGURABLE_TOOLSETS'` to find last real usage
4. **Review** whether `_callbacks` import was truly unused or merely not imported at the top level

These issues should be corrected before merging to main.

+---
+name: desloppify
+description: >
+  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker. Use when the user asks
+  about code quality, technical debt, dead code, large files, god classes,
+  duplicate functions, code smells, naming issues, import cycles, or coupling
+  problems. Also use when asked for a health score, what to fix next, or to
+  create a cleanup plan. Supports 29 languages.
+allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
+---
+
+<!-- desloppify-begin -->
+<!-- desloppify-skill-version: 5 -->
+
+# Desloppify
+
+## 1. Your Job
+
+Maximise the **strict score** honestly. Your main cycle: **scan → plan → execute → rescan**. Follow the scan output's **INSTRUCTIONS FOR AGENTS** — don't substitute your own analysis.
+
+**Don't be lazy.** Do large refactors and small detailed fixes with equal energy. If it takes touching 20 files, touch 20 files. If it's a one-line change, make it. No task is too big or too small — fix things properly, not minimally.
+
+## 2. The Workflow
+
+Three phases, repeated as a cycle.
+
+### Phase 1: Scan and review — understand the codebase
+
+```bash
+desloppify scan --path .       # analyse the codebase
+desloppify status              # check scores — are we at target?
+```
+
+The scan will tell you if subjective dimensions need review. Follow its instructions. To trigger a review manually:
+```bash
+desloppify review --prepare    # then follow your runner's review workflow
+```
+
+### Phase 2: Plan — decide what to work on
+
+After reviews, triage stages and plan creation appear in the execution queue surfaced by `next`. Complete them in order — `next` tells you what each stage expects in the `--report`:
+```bash
+desloppify next                                        # shows the next execution workflow step
+desloppify plan triage --stage observe --report "themes and root causes..."
+desloppify plan triage --stage reflect --report "comparison against completed work..."
+desloppify plan triage --stage organize --report "summary of priorities..."
+desloppify plan triage --complete --strategy "execution plan..."
+```
+
+For automated triage: `desloppify plan triage --run-stages --runner codex` (Codex) or `--runner claude` (Claude). Options: `--only-stages`, `--dry-run`, `--stage-timeout-seconds`.
+
+Then shape the queue. **The plan shapes everything `next` gives you** — `next` is the execution queue, not the full backlog. Don't skip this step.
+
+```bash
+desloppify plan                          # see the living plan details
+desloppify plan queue                    # compact execution queue view
+desloppify plan reorder <pat> top        # reorder — what unblocks the most?
+desloppify plan cluster create <name>    # group related issues to batch-fix
+desloppify plan focus <cluster>          # scope next to one cluster
+desloppify plan skip <pat>              # defer — hide from next
+```
+
+### Phase 3: Execute — grind the queue to completion
+
+Trust the plan and execute. Don't rescan mid-queue — finish the queue first.
+
+**Branch first.** Create a dedicated branch — never commit health work directly to main:
+```bash
+git checkout -b desloppify/code-health    # or desloppify/<focus-area>
+desloppify config set commit_pr 42        # link a PR for auto-updated descriptions
+```
+
+**The loop:**
+```bash
+# 1. Get the next item from the execution queue
+desloppify next
+
+# 2. Fix the issue in code
+
+# 3. Resolve it (next shows the exact command including required attestation)
+
+# 4. When you have a logical batch, commit and record
+git add <files> && git commit -m "desloppify: fix 3 deferred_import findings"
+desloppify plan commit-log record      # moves findings uncommitted → committed, updates PR
+
+# 5. Push periodically
+git push -u origin desloppify/code-health
+
+# 6. Repeat until the queue is empty
+```
+
+Score may temporarily drop after fixes — cascade effects are normal, keep going.
+If `next` suggests an auto-fixer, run `desloppify autofix <fixer> --dry-run` to preview, then apply.
+
+**When the queue is clear, go back to Phase 1.** New issues will surface, cascades will have resolved, priorities will have shifted. This is the cycle.
+
+## 3. Reference
+
+### Key concepts
+
+- **Tiers**: T1 auto-fix → T2 quick manual → T3 judgment call → T4 major refactor.
+- **Auto-clusters**: related findings are auto-grouped in `next`. Drill in with `next --cluster <name>`.
+- **Zones**: production/script (scored), test/config/generated/vendor (not scored). Fix with `zone set`.
+- **Wontfix cost**: widens the lenient↔strict gap. Challenge past decisions when the gap grows.
+
+### Scoring
+
+Overall score = **25% mechanical** + **75% subjective**.
+
+- **Mechanical (25%)**: auto-detected issues — duplication, dead code, smells, unused imports, security. Fixed by changing code and rescanning.
+- **Subjective (75%)**: design quality review — naming, error handling, abstractions, clarity. Starts at **0%** until reviewed. The scan will prompt you when a review is needed.
+- **Strict score** is the north star: wontfix items count as open. The gap between overall and strict is your wontfix debt.
+- **Score types**: overall (lenient), strict (wontfix counts), objective (mechanical only), verified (confirmed fixes only).
+
+### Reviews
+
+Four paths to get subjective scores:
+
+- **Local runner (Codex)**: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` — automated end-to-end.
+- **Local runner (Claude)**: `desloppify review --prepare` → launch parallel subagents → `desloppify review --import merged.json` — see skill doc overlay for details.
+- **Cloud/external**: `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude` → follow session template → `--external-submit`.
+- **Manual path**: `desloppify review --prepare` → review per dimension → `desloppify review --import file.json`.
+
+- Import first, fix after — import creates tracked state entries for correlation.
+- Target-matching scores trigger auto-reset to prevent gaming. Use the blind-review workflow described in your agent overlay doc (e.g. `docs/CLAUDE.md`, `docs/HERMES.md`).
+- Even moderate scores (60-80) dramatically improve overall health.
+- Stale dimensions auto-surface in `next` — just follow the queue.
+
+**Integrity rules:** Score from evidence only — no prior chat context, score history, or target-threshold anchoring. When evidence is mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty. Assess every requested dimension; never drop one.
+
+#### Review output format
+
+Return machine-readable JSON for review imports. For `--external-submit`, include `session` from the generated template:
+
+```json
+{
+  "session": {
+    "id": "<session_id_from_template>",
+    "token": "<session_token_from_template>"
+  },
+  "assessments": {
+    "<dimension_from_query>": 0
+  },
+  "findings": [
+    {
+      "dimension": "<dimension_from_query>",
+      "identifier": "short_id",
+      "summary": "one-line defect summary",
+      "related_files": ["relative/path/to/file.py"],
+      "evidence": ["specific code observation"],
+      "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",
+      "confidence": "high|medium|low"
+    }
+  ]
+}
+```
+
+`findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly (including `related_files`, `evidence`, and `suggestion`). Use `"findings": []` when no defects found. Import is fail-closed: invalid findings abort unless `--allow-partial` is passed. Assessment scores are auto-applied from trusted internal or cloud session imports. Legacy `--attested-external` remains supported.
+

   125→# =========================================================================
   126→
   127→def get_available_skills() -> Dict[str, List[str]]:
   128→    """Scan ~/.hermes/skills/ and return skills grouped by category."""
   129→    import os
   130→
   131→    hermes_home = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes"))
   132→    skills_dir = hermes_home / "skills"
   133→    skills_by_category = {}
   134→
   135→    if not skills_dir.exists():
   136→        return skills_by_category
   137→
   138→    for skill_file in skills_dir.rglob("SKILL.md"):
   139→        rel_path = skill_file.relative_to(skills_dir)
   140→        parts = rel_path.parts
   141→        if len(parts) >= 2:
   142→            category = parts[0]
   143→            skill_name = parts[-2]
   144→        else:
   145→            category = "general"
   146→            skill_name = skill_file.parent.name
   147→        skills_by_category.setdefault(category, []).append(skill_name)
   148→
   149→    return skills_by_category
   150→
   151→
   152→# =========================================================================
   153→# Update check
   154→# =========================================================================
   155→
   156→# Cache update check results for 6 hours to avoid repeated git fetches
   157→_UPDATE_CHECK_CACHE_SECONDS = 6 * 3600
   158→
   159→
   160→def check_for_updates() -> Optional[int]:
   161→    """Check how many commits behind origin/main the local repo is.
   162→
   163→    Does a ``git fetch`` at most once every 6 hours (cached to
   164→    ``~/.hermes/.update_check``).  Returns the number of commits behind,
   165→    or ``None`` if the check fails or isn't applicable.
   166→    """
   167→    hermes_home = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes"))
   168→    repo_dir = hermes_home / "hermes-agent"
   169→    cache_file = hermes_home / ".update_check"
   170→
   171→    # Must be a git repo
   172→    if not (repo_dir / ".git").exists():
   173→        return None
   174→
   175→    # Read cache
   176→    now = time.time()
   177→    try:
   178→        if cache_file.exists():
   179→            cached = json.loads(cache_file.read_text())
   180→            if now - cached.get("ts", 0) < _UPDATE_CHECK_CACHE_SECONDS:
   181→                return cached.get("behind")
   182→    except Exception:
   183→        pass
   184→
   185→    # Fetch latest refs (fast — only downloads ref metadata, no files)
   186→    try:
   187→        subprocess.run(
   188→            ["git", "fetch", "origin", "--quiet"],
   189→            capture_output=True, timeout=10,
   190→            cwd=str(repo_dir),
   191→        )
   192→    except Exception:
   193→        pass  # Offline or timeout — use stale refs, that's fine
   194→
   195→    # Count commits behind
   196→    try:
   197→        result = subprocess.run(
   198→            ["git", "rev-list", "--count", "HEAD..origin/main"],
   199→            capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5,
   200→            cwd=str(repo_dir),
   201→        )
   202→        if result.returncode == 0:
   203→            behind = int(result.stdout.strip())
   204→        else:
   205→            behind = None
   206→    except Exception:
   207→        behind = None
   208→
   209→    # Write cache
   210→    try:
   211→        cache_file.write_text(json.dumps({"ts": now, "behind": behind}))
   212→    except Exception:
   213→        pass
   214→
   215→    return behind
   216→
   217→
   218→# =========================================================================
   219→# Welcome banner
   220→# =========================================================================
   221→
   222→def _format_context_length(tokens: int) -> str:
   223→    """Format a token count for display (e.g. 128000 → '128K', 1048576 → '1M')."""
   224→    if tokens >= 1_000_000:
   225→        val = tokens / 1_000_000
   226→        return f"{val:g}M"
   227→    elif tokens >= 1_000:
   228→        val = tokens / 1_000
   229→        return f"{val:g}K"
   230→    return str(tokens)
   231→
   232→
   233→def build_welcome_banner(console: Console, model: str, cwd: str,
   234→                         tools: List[dict] = None,
   235→                         enabled_toolsets: List[str] = None,
   236→                         session_id: str = None,
   237→                         get_toolset_for_tool=None,
   238→                         context_length: int = None):
   239→    """Build and print a welcome banner with caduceus on left and info on right.
   240→
   241→    Args:
   242→        console: Rich Console instance.
   243→        model: Current model name.
   244→        cwd: Current working directory.
   245→        tools: List of tool definitions.
   246→        enabled_toolsets: List of enabled toolset names.
   247→        session_id: Session identifier.
   248→        get_toolset_for_tool: Callable to map tool name -> toolset name.
   249→        context_length: Model's context window size in tokens.
   250→    """
   251→    from model_tools import check_tool_availability
   252→    if get_toolset_for_tool is None:
   253→        from model_tools import get_toolset_for_tool
   254→
   255→    tools = tools or []
   256→    enabled_toolsets = enabled_toolsets or []
   257→
   258→    _, unavailable_toolsets = check_tool_availability(quiet=True)
   259→    disabled_tools = set()
   260→    for item in unavailable_toolsets:
   261→        disabled_tools.update(item.get("tools", []))
   262→
   263→    layout_table = Table.grid(padding=(0, 2))
   264→    layout_table.add_column("left", justify="center")
   265→    layout_table.add_column("right", justify="left")
   266→
   267→    # Resolve skin colors once for the entire banner
   268→    accent = _skin_color("banner_accent", "#FFBF00")
   269→    dim = _skin_color("banner_dim", "#B8860B")
   270→    text = _skin_color("banner_text", "#FFF8DC")
   271→    session_color = _skin_color("session_border", "#8B8682")
   272→
   273→
   274→    # Use skin's banner_hero if available, else dynamic caduceus
   275→    try:
   276→        from hermes_cli.skin_engine import get_active_skin
   277→        _hero = get_active_skin().banner_hero
   278→    except Exception:
   279→        _hero = ""
   280→    left_lines = ["", _hero or _build_hermes_caduceus(), ""]
   281→    model_short = model.split("/")[-1] if "/" in model else model
   282→    if len(model_short) > 28:
   283→        model_short = model_short[:25] + "..."
   284→    ctx_str = f" [dim {dim}]·[/] [dim {dim}]{_format_context_length(context_length)} context[/]" if context_length else ""
   285→    left_lines.append(f"[{accent}]{model_short}[/]{ctx_str} [dim {dim}]·[/] [dim {dim}]Nous Research[/]")
   286→    left_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}]{cwd}[/]")
   287→    if session_id:
   288→        left_lines.append(f"[dim {session_color}]Session: {session_id}[/]")
   289→    left_content = "\n".join(left_lines)
   290→
   291→    right_lines = [f"[bold {accent}]Available Tools[/]"]
   292→    toolsets_dict: Dict[str, list] = {}
   293→
   294→    for tool in tools:
   295→        tool_name = tool["function"]["name"]
   296→        toolset = get_toolset_for_tool(tool_name) or "other"
   297→        toolsets_dict.setdefault(toolset, []).append(tool_name)
   298→
   299→    for item in unavailable_toolsets:
   300→        toolset_id = item.get("id", item.get("name", "unknown"))
   301→        display_name = f"{toolset_id}_tools" if not toolset_id.endswith("_tools") else toolset_id
   302→        if display_name not in toolsets_dict:
   303→            toolsets_dict[display_name] = []
   304→        for tool_name in item.get("tools", []):
   305→            if tool_name not in toolsets_dict[display_name]:
   306→                toolsets_dict[display_name].append(tool_name)
   307→
   308→    sorted_toolsets = sorted(toolsets_dict.keys())
   309→    display_toolsets = sorted_toolsets[:8]
   310→    remaining_toolsets = len(sorted_toolsets) - 8
   311→
   312→    for toolset in display_toolsets:
   313→        tool_names = toolsets_dict[toolset]
   314→        colored_names = []
   315→        for name in sorted(tool_names):
   316→            if name in disabled_tools:
   317→                colored_names.append(f"[red]{name}[/]")
   318→            else:
   319→                colored_names.append(f"[{text}]{name}[/]")
   320→
   321→        tools_str = ", ".join(colored_names)
   322→        if len(", ".join(sorted(tool_names))) > 45:
   323→            short_names = []
   324→            length = 0
   325→            for name in sorted(tool_names):
   326→                if length + len(name) + 2 > 42:
   327→                    short_names.append("...")
   328→                    break
   329→                short_names.append(name)
   330→                length += len(name) + 2
   331→            colored_names = []
   332→            for name in short_names:
   333→                if name == "...":
   334→                    colored_names.append("[dim]...[/]")
   335→                elif name in disabled_tools:
   336→                    colored_names.append(f"[red]{name}[/]")
   337→                else:
   338→                    colored_names.append(f"[{text}]{name}[/]")
   339→            tools_str = ", ".join(colored_names)
   340→
   341→        right_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}]{toolset}:[/] {tools_str}")
   342→
   343→    if remaining_toolsets > 0:
   344→        right_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}](and {remaining_toolsets} more toolsets...)[/]")
   345→
   346→    # MCP Servers section (only if configured)
   347→    try:
   348→        from tools.mcp_tool import get_mcp_status
   349→        mcp_status = get_mcp_status()
   350→    except Exception:
   351→        mcp_status = []
   352→
   353→    if mcp_status:
   354→        right_lines.append("")
   355→        right_lines.append(f"[bold {accent}]MCP Servers[/]")
   356→        for srv in mcp_status:
   357→            if srv["connected"]:
   358→                right_lines.append(
   359→                    f"[dim {dim}]{srv['name']}[/] [{text}]({srv['transport']})[/] "
   360→                    f"[dim {dim}]—[/] [{text}]{srv['tools']} tool(s)[/]"
   361→                )
   362→            else:
   363→                right_lines.append(
   364→                    f"[red]{srv['name']}[/] [dim]({srv['transport']})[/] "
   365→                    f"[red]— failed[/]"
   366→                )
   367→
   368→    right_lines.append("")
   369→    right_lines.append(f"[bold {accent}]Available Skills[/]")
   370→    skills_by_category = get_available_skills()
   371→    total_skills = sum(len(s) for s in skills_by_category.values())
   372→
   373→    if skills_by_category:
   374→        for category in sorted(skills_by_category.keys()):
   375→            skill_names = sorted(skills_by_category[category])
   376→            if len(skill_names) > 8:
   377→                display_names = skill_names[:8]
   378→                skills_str = ", ".join(display_names) + f" +{len(skill_names) - 8} more"
   379→            else:
   380→                skills_str = ", ".join(skill_names)
   381→            if len(skills_str) > 50:
   382→                skills_str = skills_str[:47] + "..."
   383→            right_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}]{category}:[/] [{text}]{skills_str}[/]")
   384→    else:
   385→        right_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}]No skills installed[/]")
   386→
   387→    right_lines.append("")
   388→    mcp_connected = sum(1 for s in mcp_status if s["connected"]) if mcp_status else 0
   389→    summary_parts = [f"{len(tools)} tools", f"{total_skills} skills"]
   390→    if mcp_connected:
   391→        summary_parts.append(f"{mcp_connected} MCP servers")
   392→    summary_parts.append("/help for commands")
   393→    right_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}]{' · '.join(summary_parts)}[/]")
   394→
   395→    # Update check — show if behind origin/main
   396→    try:
   397→        behind = check_for_updates()
   398→        if behind and behind > 0:
   399→            commits_word = "commit" if behind == 1 else "commits"
   400→            right_lines.append(
   401→                f"[bold yellow]⚠ {behind} {commits_word} behind[/]"
   402→                f"[dim yellow] — run [bold]hermes update[/bold] to update[/]"
   403→            )
   404→    except Exception:
   405→        pass  # Never break the banner over an update check
   406→
   407→    right_content = "\n".join(right_lines)
   408→    layout_table.add_row(left_content, right_content)
   409→
   410→    agent_name = _skin_branding("agent_name", "Hermes Agent")
   411→    title_color = _skin_color("banner_title", "#FFD700")
   412→    border_color = _skin_color("banner_border", "#CD7F32")
   413→    outer_panel = Panel(
   414→        layout_table,
   415→        title=f"[bold {title_color}]{agent_name} v{VERSION} ({RELEASE_DATE})[/]",
   416→        border_style=border_color,
   417→        padding=(0, 2),
   418→    )
   419→
   420→    # Use skin's banner_logo if available, else dynamic logo
   421→    try:
   422→        from hermes_cli.skin_engine import get_active_skin
   423→        _logo = get_active_skin().banner_logo
   424→    except Exception:
   425→        _logo = ""
   426→
   427→    console.print()
   428→    console.print(_logo or _build_hermes_logo())
   429→    console.print()
   430→    console.print(outer_panel)
   431→

```
### Review workflow

Invoke the reviewer agent for each group of dimensions sequentially.
Even without parallelism, isolating dimensions across separate agent
invocations prevents score bleed between concerns.

Merge assessments and findings, then import.

When Gemini CLI adds parallel subagent execution, split dimensions across
concurrent agent calls instead.

<!-- desloppify-overlay: gemini -->
<!-- desloppify-end -->

## Hermes Agent Overlay

Hermes has built-in parallel subagent support via `delegate_task` (up to 3
concurrent children). Use batch mode for review dimensions.

### Review workflow

1. **Prepare prompts:**
```bash
desloppify review --run-batches --dry-run
```
This generates per-batch prompt files in `.desloppify/subagents/runs/<run-id>/prompts/`
and prints the run directory path. Note the run directory — you need it for every step.

2. **Launch subagents in groups of 3** using `delegate_task` with the `tasks` array.
Each subagent reads its prompt file, inspects the repository, and writes its
result. Subagents must not edit repository source files — only write the result.

Output filename is `batch-N.raw.txt` (not `.json`) — the importer requires this exact name.

Example `delegate_task` call:
```
delegate_task(tasks=[
  {
    "goal": "Review batch 1. Read the prompt at .desloppify/subagents/runs/<run-id>/prompts/batch-1.md, follow it exactly, inspect the repository, and write ONLY valid JSON to .desloppify/subagents/runs/<run-id>/results/batch-1.raw.txt.",
    "context": "Repository root: <cwd>. Blind packet: .desloppify/review_packet_blind.json. The prompt file defines the required output schema. Do not edit repository source files. Only write the review result file.",
```

## Critical Review of Commits 9309c4f and 8058d57

### **CRITICAL ISSUE 1: _run_cleanup() Control Flow Bug - cli.py:449, 453, 458**

**Severity: HIGH - Functions silently fail**

The refactoring changed exception handlers from `pass` to `return None`. This introduces a **control flow bug**:

```python
def _run_cleanup():
    """Run resource cleanup exactly once."""
    global _cleanup_done
    if _cleanup_done:
        return
    _cleanup_done = True
    try:
        _cleanup_all_terminals()
    except Exception:
        return None  # BUG: Returns early, skips remaining cleanup
    try:
        _cleanup_all_browsers()
    except Exception:
        return None  # BUG: Returns early, skips MCP shutdown
    try:
        from tools.mcp_tool import shutdown_mcp_servers
        shutdown_mcp_servers()
    except Exception:
        return None
```

If `_cleanup_all_terminals()` fails (line 449), the function returns immediately without running `_cleanup_all_browsers()` or `shutdown_mcp_servers()`. The original `pass` statements allowed execution to continue to the next try block. **This is a regression that breaks cleanup logic.**

**Fix:** Use `pass` or restructure with `continue` in a loop/context, or consolidate all exceptions and log them.

---

### **CRITICAL ISSUE 2: AGENTS.md Duplicate Agent Blocks - AGENTS.md:350-381**

**Severity: HIGH - Malformed config**

Three **identical** `desloppify` agent config blocks appear consecutively (lines 350-359, 361-370, 372-381) with no content between them:

```markdown
---
name: desloppify
description: >
  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker...
allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
---

---
name: desloppify
description: >
  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker...
allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
---

---
name: desloppify
description: >
  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker...
allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
---
```

This violates agent config syntax. Parser behavior is undefined — it may register only the first, the last, or all three (causing runtime errors). The commit message claims this "adds desloppify agent config" but includes **3 copies** with **no explanation** for the duplication.

---

### **MODERATE ISSUE 3: Removed Imports Used Elsewhere - cli.py:424, 426**

**Severity: MODERATE - Missing unused imports may mask real issues**

The commit removed from cli.py (lines 424, 426 in original):
- `from hermes_cli.commands import COMMANDS` (removed, not used)
- `from hermes_cli import callbacks as _callbacks` (removed, not used)
- `from toolsets import resolve_toolset` (removed from cli.py)

Grep confirms `COMMANDS` is not used in cli.py, but the module name is suspicious. The removal of `_callbacks` requires verification that callback registration isn't silently broken elsewhere.

**Low risk** per grep output (no references found), but the pattern suggests incomplete refactoring.

---

### **MODERATE ISSUE 4: tools_config.py Dead Code Removal - Unverified**

**Severity: LOW-MODERATE - Possibly safe, but not audited**

The commit removed 196 lines of "large unused configurator UI block" including:
- `_prompt_yes_no()` function (lines 57-68)
- `CONFIGURABLE_TOOLSETS`, `_DEFAULT_OFF_TOOLSETS`, `PLATFORMS`, `TOOL_CATEGORIES`, `TOOLSET_ENV_REQUIREMENTS` dicts
- Post-setup hooks infrastructure

**The commit message claims this is "dead code"**, but:
1. No evidence provided that these dicts aren't referenced in `_configure_tool_category()` or `_configure_provider()` (which remain in the file)
2. These look like **intentionally disabled UI code**, not dead code
3. **No audit trail** showing grep/rg results confirming zero references

**Grep verification** of the remaining code shows references to `cat.get("setup_title")`, `provider.get("name")`, etc., which suggests the removed dicts may have been the **source** of that data in previous code paths.

---

### **MINOR ISSUE 5: Misaligned Import Removal in hermes_cli/main.py - main.py:727**

**Severity: LOW - Deferred import catches it**

The commit removed `save_config` from line 727's imports in `cmd_model()`:
```python
-    from hermes_cli.config import load_config, save_config, get_env_value, save_env_value
+    from hermes_cli.config import load_config, get_env_value
```

However, `save_config` **is still used at line 940** (inside a nested function). The removal doesn't break because:
- Line 932 re-imports it locally: `from hermes_cli.config import load_config, save_config`
- But this is **fragile** and **sloppy** — imports should be at function scope, not scattered

**Better pattern:** Keep the import at function scope consistently.

---

### **MINOR ISSUE 6: shutil.which() "Improvement" - cli.py:475, 525, 591, 607, 616, 657, 668, 674, 679**

**Severity: LOW - Minimal practical value**

The commit changed:
```python
-            ["git", "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
+            [shutil.which("git") or "git", "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
```

Repeated 9 times across `_git_repo_root()`, `_setup_worktree()`, `_check_worktree_active()`, and `_cleanup_worktree()`.

**Analysis:**
- `shutil.which("git")` returns `None` if git is not in PATH
- Fallback `or "git"` is used when `which()` fails
- **Problem:** If git is not in PATH, subprocess will still fail with the bare `"git"` command
- **Improvement is nominal:** In practice, if git isn't in PATH, the code fails either way
- **No actual benefit:** The try/except already handles subprocess failures gracefully

This is **noise masquerading as improvement** — it doesn't strengthen error handling.

---

| Issue | File | Line(s) | Severity | Type | Action Required |

| 1 | cli.py | 449, 453, 458 | **CRITICAL** | Logic bug | Revert `return None` to `pass` |
| 2 | AGENTS.md | 350-381 | **CRITICAL** | Malformed config | Remove 2 of 3 duplicate blocks |
| 3 | cli.py | 424, 426 | MODERATE | Removed imports | Verify `_callbacks` not used elsewhere |
| 4 | tools_config.py | (196 lines) | MODERATE | Unverified dead code removal | Audit against actual usage or restore |
| 5 | hermes_cli/main.py | 727, 932 | LOW | Fragmented imports | Consolidate imports at function scope |
| 6 | cli.py | 475, 525, 591+ | LOW | Cosmetic "improvement" | Safe to keep, but adds no value |

---

### **Recommendations**

1. **Immediately revert** commit 9309c4f changes to `_run_cleanup()` (lines 449, 453, 458)
2. **Fix** commit 8058d57 by removing duplicate desloppify blocks from AGENTS.md
3. **Audit** tools_config.py removal by running: `git log -p --all -S 'CONFIGURABLE_TOOLSETS'` to find last real usage
4. **Review** whether `_callbacks` import was truly unused or merely not imported at the top level

These issues should be corrected before merging to main.

"""
Session Insights Engine for Hermes Agent.

Analyzes historical session data from the SQLite state database to produce
comprehensive usage insights — token consumption, cost estimates, tool usage
patterns, activity trends, model/platform breakdowns, and session metrics.

Inspired by Claude Code's /insights command, adapted for Hermes Agent's
multi-platform architecture with additional cost estimation and platform
breakdown capabilities.

Usage:
    from agent.insights import InsightsEngine
    engine = InsightsEngine(db)
    report = engine.generate(days=30)
    print(engine.format_terminal(report))
"""

import json
import time
from collections import Counter, defaultdict
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional

# =========================================================================
# Model pricing (USD per million tokens) — approximate as of early 2026
# =========================================================================
MODEL_PRICING = {
    # OpenAI
    "gpt-4o": {"input": 2.50, "output": 10.00},

```
"""
GLM 4.7 tool call parser.

Same as GLM 4.5 but with slightly different regex patterns.
The tool_call tags may wrap differently and arg parsing handles
newlines between key/value pairs.

Based on VLLM's Glm47MoeModelToolParser (extends Glm4MoeModelToolParser).
"""

import re

from environments.tool_call_parsers import register_parser
from environments.tool_call_parsers.glm45_parser import Glm45ToolCallParser


@register_parser("glm47")
class Glm47ToolCallParser(Glm45ToolCallParser):
    """
    Parser for GLM 4.7 tool calls.
    Extends GLM 4.5 with updated regex patterns.
    """

    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        # GLM 4.7 uses a slightly different detail regex that includes
        # the <tool_call> wrapper and optional arg_key content
        self.FUNC_DETAIL_REGEX = re.compile(
            r"<tool_call>(.*?)(<arg_key>.*?)?</tool_call>", re.DOTALL
        )
        # GLM 4.7 handles newlines between arg_key and arg_value tags
        self.FUNC_ARG_REGEX = re.compile(
            r"<arg_key>(.*?)</arg_key>(?:\\n|\s)*<arg_value>(.*?)</arg_value>",
            re.DOTALL,
        )
```

## Commit 52695d7 Analysis Report

After detailed examination of the full diff for commit 52695d7, I have completed a comprehensive review of:

1. **Unused import removals** - Verified approximately 50+ imports were removed
2. **F-string to string literal conversions** - Checked for interpolation safety
3. **Function/class removals** - Identified and validated
4. **Type hint removals** - Verified against actual code usage

### Findings

**RESULT: All changes are correct. No correctness issues found.**

Details:

**Import removals verified as safe:**
- `json`, `os`, `asyncio` from various files - confirmed unused in code
- `uuid` from kimi_k2_parser.py, mistral_parser.py - confirmed unused
- Type hints (`Optional`, `Set`, `Union`, `Any`, `Literal`, `Tuple`) removed from files - verified none are used in type annotations
- `ServerManager` from hermes_base_env.py - confirmed unused
- `ScoredDataItem` from agentic_opd_env.py - confirmed unused
- `ParseResult` from glm47_parser.py - confirmed unused
- `SessionResetPolicy` from gateway/session.py - confirmed unused
- `timezone as _tz` from hermes_time.py - confirmed the alias `_tz` is not used (different from variables like `_cached_tz`)
- `cache_image_from_url`, `cache_audio_from_url` from gateway/platforms/slack.py - confirmed unused
- `field` from gateway/session.py - confirmed unused

**F-string conversions verified as safe:**
- All 40+ bare f-string conversions in honcho_integration/cli.py and agent/insights.py contain no variable interpolation (`{...}` patterns)
- No f-strings with actual interpolation were converted

**Function deletion verified:**
- `_get_session_name()` from tools/browser_tool.py - confirmed this private function was never called

**Conclusion:** This is a pure lint/style cleanup commit with zero functional changes or risk of breaking anything.
