    79→            "session_key": key,
    80→            "status": "running",
    81→            "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
    82→            "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
    83→        })
    84→
    85→    async def switch_model(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    86→        """Switch the model on a live agent session.
    87→
    88→        POST body: {"provider": "...", "model": "...", "reason": "..."}
    89→
    90→        If session_key is "_any", switches the first running agent found.
    91→        """
    92→        [REDACTED]["key"]
    93→
    94→        try:
    95→            body = await request.json()
    96→        except Exception:
    97→            return _json_response({"error": "Invalid JSON body"}, status=400)
    98→
    99→        provider = body.get("provider", "").strip()
   100→        model = body.get("model", "").strip()
   101→
   102→        if not provider or not model:
   103→            return _json_response(
   104→                {"error": "Both 'provider' and 'model' are required"},
   105→                status=400,
   106→            )
   107→
   108→        # _any: switch whichever agent is running
   109→        if key == "_any":
   110→            if not self.runner._running_agents:
   111→                return _json_response(
   112→                    {"error": "No running agents"},
   113→                    status=404,
   114→                )
   115→            key = next(iter(self.runner._running_agents))
   116→
   117→        agent = self.runner._running_agents.get(key)
   118→        if not agent:
   119→            return _json_response(
   120→                {"error": f"No running agent for session '{key}'"},
   121→                status=404,
   122→            )
   123→
   124→        reason = body.get("reason", "external control API")
   125→        logger.info(
   126→            "Control API: switch_model session=%s → %s/%s (reason: %s)",
   127→            key, provider, model, reason,
   128→        )
   129→
   130→        # Defer the switch so it happens between iterations, not mid-API-call.
   131→        # If the agent has the deferred mechanism, use it; otherwise switch directly.
   132→        if hasattr(agent, "_pending_model_switch"):
   133→            agent._pending_model_switch = {"provider": provider, "model": model}
   134→            return _json_response({
   135→                "success": True,
   136→                "message": "Model switch queued — will apply before next API call",
   137→                "current": {"provider": getattr(agent, "provider", ""), "model": getattr(agent, "model", "")},
   138→                "target": {"provider": provider, "model": model},
   139→            })
   140→        else:
   141→            result = agent._switch_model(provider, model)
   142→            status = 200 if result.get("success") else 500
   143→            return _json_response(result, status=status)
   144→
   145→    # ── Lifecycle ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   146→
   147→    async def start(self):
   148→        port = _get_port()
   149→        app_runner = web.AppRunner(self.app, access_log=None)
   150→        await app_runner.setup()
   151→        self._site = web.TCPSite(app_runner, "127.0.0.1", port)
   152→        try:
   153→            await self._site.start()
   154→            logger.info("Control API listening on http://127.0.0.1:%d", port)
   155→            # Write port file so external tools can discover us
   156→            self._write_port_file(port)
   157→        except OSError as e:
   158→            logger.warning("Control API failed to bind port %d: %s", port, e)
   159→
   160→    async def stop(self):
   161→        if self._site:
   162→            await self._site.stop()
   163→            self._remove_port_file()
   164→            logger.info("Control API stopped")
   165→
   166→    @staticmethod
   167→    def _write_port_file(port: int):
   168→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   169→        try:
   170→            with open(path, "w") as f:
   171→                f.write(str(port))
   172→        except OSError:
   173→            pass
   174→
   175→    @staticmethod
   176→    def _remove_port_file():
   177→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   178→        try:
   179→            os.remove(path)
   180→        except OSError:
   181→            pass
   182→

1→"""
     2→Hermes control client — lightweight module for external tools to control
     3→a running Hermes gateway.
     4→
     5→This is the module desloppify (or any external tool) imports to switch
     6→models, list sessions, etc. on a live Hermes instance.
     7→
     8→Usage:
     9→    from gateway.hermes_control_client import HermesControl
    10→
    11→    ctl = HermesControl()  # auto-discovers port from ~/.hermes/control_api.port
    12→
    13→    # Switch model on whichever agent is running
    14→    result = ctl.switch_model("openrouter", "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4")
    15→
    16→    # Switch model on a specific session
    17→    result = ctl.switch_model("openrouter", "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4",
    18→                              session_key="agent:main:telegram:12345")
    19→
    20→    # List running sessions
    21→    sessions = ctl.list_sessions()
    22→
    23→Zero dependencies beyond stdlib (uses urllib, not requests/aiohttp).
    24→"""
    25→
    26→import json
    27→import os
    28→import urllib.request
    29→import urllib.error
    30→from typing import Optional
    31→
    32→DEFAULT_PORT = 47823
    33→PORT_FILE = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
    34→
    35→
    36→class HermesControl:
    37→    """Client for the Hermes gateway control API."""
    38→
    39→    def __init__(self, host: str = "127.0.0.1", port: Optional[int] = None):
    40→        if port is None:
    41→            port = self._discover_port()
    42→        self.base_url = f"http://{host}:{port}"
    43→
    44→    @staticmethod
    45→    def _discover_port() -> int:
    46→        try:
    47→            with open(PORT_FILE) as f:
    48→                return int(f.read().strip())
    49→        except (OSError, ValueError):
    50→            return DEFAULT_PORT
    51→
    52→    def _request(self, method: str, path: str, body: dict = None) -> dict:
    53→        url = f"{self.base_url}{path}"
    54→        data = json.dumps(body).encode() if body else None
    55→        req = urllib.request.Request(
    56→            url,
    57→            data=data,
    58→            method=method,
    59→            headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"} if data else {},
    60→        )
    61→        try:
    62→            with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=5) as resp:
    63→                return json.loads(resp.read())
    64→        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
    65→            return json.loads(e.read())
    66→        except urllib.error.URLError as e:
    67→            return {"error": f"Cannot reach Hermes control API: {e.reason}"}
    68→
    69→    def health(self) -> dict:
    70→        """Check if the control API is running."""
    71→        return self._request("GET", "/health")
    72→
    73→    def list_sessions(self) -> dict:
    74→        """List all running agent sessions."""
    75→        return self._request("GET", "/sessions")
    76→
    77→    def get_session(self, session_key: str) -> dict:
    78→        """Get info about a specific session."""
    79→        return self._request("GET", f"/sessions/{session_key}")
    80→
    81→    def switch_model(
    82→        self,
    83→        provider: str,
    84→        model: str,
    85→        session_key: str = "_any",
    86→        reason: str = "",
    87→    ) -> dict:
    88→        """Switch the model on a running agent session.
    89→
    90→        Args:
    91→            provider: Provider ID (e.g., "openrouter", "anthropic")
    92→            model: Model ID (e.g., "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4")
    93→            session_key: Session to switch. "_any" switches the first running agent.
    94→            reason: Optional reason string (logged by Hermes).
    95→
    96→        Returns:
    97→            Dict with success/failure and previous/current model info.
    98→        """
    99→        return self._request("POST", f"/sessions/{session_key}/switch-model", {
   100→            "provider": provider,
   101→            "model": model,
   102→            "reason": reason or "desloppify",
   103→        })
   104→
   105→    def is_available(self) -> bool:
   106→        """Check if a Hermes gateway with control API is reachable."""
   107→        try:
   108→            result = self.health()
   109→            return result.get("status") == "ok"
   110→        except Exception:
   111→            return False
   112→

1→"""
     2→switch_model tool — lets the agent discover available models/providers
     3→and switch to a different one mid-conversation.
     4→
     5→Two actions:
     6→  - list: show current model, original model, and available providers+models
     7→  - switch: change to a different provider/model at runtime
     8→"""
     9→
    10→import json
    11→import logging
    12→
    13→from tools.registry import registry
    14→
    15→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    16→
    17→
    18→def switch_model_list(
    19→    current_provider: str,
    20→    current_model: str,
    21→    original: dict,
    22→) -> str:
    23→    """Return JSON describing current state and available providers/models."""
    24→    from hermes_cli.models import list_available_providers, curated_models_for_provider
    25→
    26→    available = []
    27→    for prov in list_available_providers():
    28→        models = curated_models_for_provider(prov["id"])
    29→        available.append({
    30→            "provider": prov["id"],
    31→            "label": prov["label"],
    32→            "authenticated": prov["authenticated"],
    33→            "models": [m_id for m_id, _ in models],
    34→        })
    35→
    36→    return json.dumps({
    37→        "current": {"provider": current_provider, "model": current_model},
    38→        "original": original,
    39→        "available_providers": available,
    40→    }, ensure_ascii=False)
    41→
    42→
    43→def switch_model_switch(
    44→    provider: str,
    45→    model: str,
    46→    reason: str,
    47→    agent,  # AIAgent — imported at call site to avoid circular import
    48→) -> str:
    49→    """Ask the agent to switch its model/provider in-place.
    50→
    51→    Delegates to agent._switch_model() which handles client construction.
    52→    Returns JSON the agent sees as its tool result.
    53→    """
    54→    if not provider or not model:
    55→        return json.dumps({
    56→            "success": False,
    57→            "error": "Both 'provider' and 'model' are required for switch action.",
    58→            "current": {"provider": agent.provider, "model": agent.model},
    59→        }, ensure_ascii=False)
    60→
    61→    if reason:
    62→        logger.info("switch_model reason: %s", reason)
    63→
    64→    result = agent._switch_model(provider, model)
    65→    return json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False)
    66→
    67→
    68→# ── Schema ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    69→
    70→SWITCH_MODEL_SCHEMA = {
    71→    "name": "switch_model",
    72→    "description": (
    73→        "Discover available LLM providers/models and switch to a different one "
    74→        "mid-conversation. Use action='list' to see what's available, then "
    75→        "action='switch' to change. Consider switching to a faster/cheaper model "
    76→        "for simple tasks, or a more capable model for complex reasoning."
    77→    ),
    78→    "parameters": {
    79→        "type": "object",
    80→        "properties": {
    81→            "action": {
    82→                "type": "string",
    83→                "enum": ["list", "switch"],
    84→                "description": "Action to perform: 'list' shows available models, 'switch' changes the active model.",
    85→            },
    86→            "provider": {
    87→                "type": "string",
    88→                "description": "Provider to switch to (required for 'switch'). E.g. 'openrouter', 'nous', 'anthropic'.",
    89→            },
    90→            "model": {
    91→                "type": "string",
    92→                "description": "Model ID to switch to (required for 'switch'). E.g. 'anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6'.",
    93→            },
    94→            "reason": {
    95→                "type": "string",
    96→                "description": "Optional: why you're switching (logged for observability).",
    97→            },
    98→        },
    99→        "required": ["action"],
   100→    },
   101→}
   102→
   103→
   104→def _check_switch_model() -> bool:
   105→    """Always available when loaded."""
   106→    return True
   107→
   108→
   109→# ── Registration ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   110→
   111→registry.register(
   112→    name="switch_model",
   113→    toolset="switch_model",
   114→    schema=SWITCH_MODEL_SCHEMA,
   115→    # Handler is a stub — actual dispatch happens in run_agent._invoke_tool()
   116→    # because this tool needs agent-level state access.
   117→    handler=lambda args, **kw: json.dumps({
   118→        "error": "switch_model must be handled by the agent loop"
   119→    }),
   120→    check_fn=_check_switch_model,
   121→)
   122→

1→"""
     2→smart_model — PoC tool that automatically picks and switches to the best model
     3→for a given task type.
     4→
     5→The agent calls this with a task description, and the tool picks an appropriate
     6→model (e.g., fast/cheap for simple tasks, powerful for complex ones) and switches.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→import json
    10→import logging
    11→
    12→from tools.registry import registry
    13→
    14→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    15→
    16→# Simple task-to-model mapping — customize as needed
    17→MODEL_PRESETS = {
    18→    "fast": {
    19→        "provider": "openrouter",
    20→        "model": "google/gemini-2.5-flash",
    21→        "description": "Fast and cheap — good for simple lookups, summaries, formatting",
    22→    },
    23→    "balanced": {
    24→        "provider": "openrouter",
    25→        "model": "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4",
    26→        "description": "Good balance of speed and capability",
    27→    },
    28→    "powerful": {
    29→        "provider": "openrouter",
    30→        "model": "anthropic/claude-opus-4",
    31→        "description": "Most capable — complex reasoning, coding, analysis",
    32→    },
    33→    "code": {
    34→        "provider": "openrouter",
    35→        "model": "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4",
    36→        "description": "Optimized for code generation and debugging",
    37→    },
    38→}
    39→
    40→
    41→def smart_model_handle(agent, preset: str, custom_provider: str = "", custom_model: str = "") -> str:
    42→    """Pick a model based on preset or custom provider/model, then switch."""
    43→
    44→    old_model = agent.model
    45→    old_provider = agent.provider
    46→
    47→    # Use custom if provided, otherwise look up preset
    48→    if custom_provider and custom_model:
    49→        provider = custom_provider
    50→        model = custom_model
    51→    elif preset in MODEL_PRESETS:
    52→        provider = MODEL_PRESETS[preset]["provider"]
    53→        model = MODEL_PRESETS[preset]["model"]
    54→    else:
    55→        return json.dumps({
    56→            "success": False,
    57→            "error": f"Unknown preset '{preset}'. Available: {list(MODEL_PRESETS.keys())}",
    58→            "current": {"provider": old_provider, "model": old_model},
    59→        })
    60→
    61→    # Already on this model? No-op.
    62→    if model == old_model and provider == old_provider:
    63→        return json.dumps({
    64→            "success": True,
    65→            "message": f"Already using {model}",
    66→            "current": {"provider": provider, "model": model},
    67→        })
    68→
    69→    # Delegate to agent's built-in switch
    70→    result = agent._switch_model(provider, model)
    71→    if result.get("success"):
    72→        result["preset"] = preset if preset in MODEL_PRESETS else "custom"
    73→    return json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False)
    74→
    75→
    76→# ── Schema ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    77→
    78→SMART_MODEL_SCHEMA = {
    79→    "name": "smart_model",
    80→    "description": (
    81→        "Switch to a model preset suited for the current task. "
    82→        "Presets: 'fast' (cheap/quick), 'balanced' (general), 'powerful' (complex reasoning), "
    83→        "'code' (programming). Or provide custom provider+model."
    84→    ),
    85→    "parameters": {
    86→        "type": "object",
    87→        "properties": {
    88→            "preset": {
    89→                "type": "string",
    90→                "enum": ["fast", "balanced", "powerful", "code", "custom"],
    91→                "description": "Model preset to use, or 'custom' with provider+model.",
    92→            },
    93→            "provider": {
    94→                "type": "string",
    95→                "description": "Custom provider (only with preset='custom'). E.g. 'openrouter'.",
    96→            },
    97→            "model": {
    98→                "type": "string",
    99→                "description": "Custom model ID (only with preset='custom'). E.g. 'openai/gpt-4o'.",
   100→            },
   101→        },
   102→        "required": ["preset"],
   103→    },
   104→}
   105→
   106→
   107→def _check_smart_model() -> bool:
   108→    return True
   109→
   110→
   111→registry.register(
   112→    name="smart_model",
   113→    toolset="smart_model",
   114→    schema=SMART_MODEL_SCHEMA,
   115→    handler=lambda args, **kw: json.dumps({
   116→        "error": "smart_model must be handled by the agent loop"
   117→    }),
   118→    check_fn=_check_smart_model,
   119→)
   120→

1→"""Central registry for all hermes-agent tools.
     2→
     3→Each tool file calls ``registry.register()`` at module level to declare its
     4→schema, handler, toolset membership, and availability check.  ``model_tools.py``
     5→queries the registry instead of maintaining its own parallel data structures.
     6→
     7→Import chain (circular-import safe):
     8→    tools/registry.py  (no imports from model_tools or tool files)
     9→           ^
    10→    tools/*.py  (import from tools.registry at module level)
    11→           ^
    12→    model_tools.py  (imports tools.registry + all tool modules)
    13→           ^
    14→    run_agent.py, cli.py, batch_runner.py, etc.
    15→"""
    16→
    17→import json
    18→import logging
    19→from typing import Callable, Dict, List, Optional, Set
    20→
    21→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    22→
    23→
    24→class ToolEntry:
    25→    """Metadata for a single registered tool."""
    26→
    27→    __slots__ = (
    28→        "name", "toolset", "schema", "handler", "check_fn",
    29→        "requires_env", "is_async", "description",
    30→    )
    31→
    32→    def __init__(self, name, toolset, schema, handler, check_fn,
    33→                 requires_env, is_async, description):
    34→        self.name = name
    35→        self.toolset = toolset
    36→        self.schema = schema
    37→        self.handler = handler
    38→        self.check_fn = check_fn
    39→        self.requires_env = requires_env
    40→        self.is_async = is_async
    41→        self.description = description
    42→
    43→
    44→class ToolRegistry:
    45→    """Singleton registry that collects tool schemas + handlers from tool files."""
    46→
    47→    def __init__(self):
    48→        self._tools: Dict[str, ToolEntry] = {}
    49→        self._toolset_checks: Dict[str, Callable] = {}
    50→
    51→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    52→    # Registration
    53→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    54→
    55→    def register(
    56→        self,
    57→        name: str,
    58→        toolset: str,
    59→        schema: dict,
    60→        handler: Callable,
    61→        check_fn: Callable = None,
    62→        requires_env: list = None,
    63→        is_async: bool = False,
    64→        description: str = "",
    65→    ):
    66→        """Register a tool.  Called at module-import time by each tool file."""
    67→        self._tools[name] = ToolEntry(
    68→            name=name,
    69→            toolset=toolset,
    70→            schema=schema,
    71→            handler=handler,
    72→            check_fn=check_fn,
    73→            requires_env=requires_env or [],
    74→            is_async=is_async,
    75→            description=description or schema.get("description", ""),
    76→        )
    77→        if check_fn and toolset not in self._toolset_checks:
    78→            self._toolset_checks[toolset] = check_fn
    79→
    80→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

     2→"""
     3→Model Tools Module
     4→
     5→Thin orchestration layer over the tool registry. Each tool file in tools/
     6→self-registers its schema, handler, and metadata via tools.registry.register().
     7→This module triggers discovery (by importing all tool modules), then provides
     8→the public API that run_agent.py, cli.py, batch_runner.py, and the RL
     9→environments consume.
    10→
    11→Public API (signatures preserved from the original 2,400-line version):
    12→    get_tool_definitions(enabled_toolsets, disabled_toolsets, quiet_mode) -> list
    13→    handle_function_call(function_name, function_args, task_id, user_task) -> str

    16→    get_all_tool_names() -> list
    17→    get_toolset_for_tool(name) -> str
    18→    get_available_toolsets() -> dict
    19→    check_toolset_requirements() -> dict
    20→    check_tool_availability(quiet) -> tuple

    38→def _run_async(coro):
    39→    """Run an async coroutine from a sync context.
    40→
    41→    If the current thread already has a running event loop (e.g., inside
    42→    the gateway's async stack or Atropos's event loop), we spin up a
    43→    disposable thread so asyncio.run() can create its own loop without
    44→    conflicting.
    45→
    46→    This is the single source of truth for sync->async bridging in tool
    47→    handlers. The RL paths (agent_loop.py, tool_context.py) also provide
    48→    outer thread-pool wrapping as defense-in-depth, but each handler is
    49→    self-protecting via this function.
    50→    """
    51→    try:
    52→        loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
    53→    except RuntimeError:
    54→        loop = None
    55→
    56→    if loop and loop.is_running():
    57→        import concurrent.futures
    58→        with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1) as pool:
    59→            future = pool.submit(asyncio.run, coro)
    60→            return future.result(timeout=300)
    61→    return asyncio.run(coro)
    62→

    77→        "tools.file_tools",

    79→        "tools.mixture_of_agents_tool",
    80→        "tools.image_generation_tool",

265→def handle_function_call(
   266→    function_name: str,
   267→    function_args: Dict[str, Any],
   268→    task_id: Optional[str] = None,
   269→    user_task: Optional[str] = None,
   270→    enabled_tools: Optional[List[str]] = None,
   271→) -> str:
   272→    """
   273→    Main function call dispatcher that routes calls to the tool registry.
   274→
   275→    Args:
   276→        function_name: Name of the function to call.
   277→        function_args: Arguments for the function.
   278→        task_id: Unique identifier for terminal/browser session isolation.
   279→        user_task: The user's original task (for browser_snapshot context).
   280→        enabled_tools: Tool names enabled for this session.  When provided,
   281→                       execute_code uses this list to determine which sandbox
   282→                       tools to generate.  Falls back to the process-global
   283→                       ``_last_resolved_tool_names`` for backward compat.
   284→
   285→    Returns:
   286→        Function result as a JSON string.
   287→    """
   288→    # Notify the read-loop tracker when a non-read/search tool runs,
   289→    # so the *consecutive* counter resets (reads after other work are fine).
   290→    _READ_SEARCH_TOOLS = {"read_file", "search_files"}
   291→    if function_name not in _READ_SEARCH_TOOLS:
   292→        try:
   293→            from tools.file_tools import notify_other_tool_call
   294→            notify_other_tool_call(task_id or "default")
   295→        except Exception:
   296→            pass  # file_tools may not be loaded yet
   297→
   298→    try:
   299→        if function_name in _AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS:
   300→            return json.dumps({"error": f"{function_name} must be handled by the agent loop"})
   301→
   302→        if function_name == "execute_code":
   303→            # Prefer the caller-provided list so subagents can't overwrite
   304→            # the parent's tool set via the process-global.
   305→            sandbox_enabled = enabled_tools if enabled_tools is not None else _last_resolved_tool_names
   306→            return registry.dispatch(
   307→                function_name, function_args,
   308→                task_id=task_id,
   309→                enabled_tools=sandbox_enabled,
   310→            )
   311→
   312→        return registry.dispatch(
   313→            function_name, function_args,
   314→            task_id=task_id,
   315→            user_task=user_task,
   316→        )
   317→
   318→    except Exception as e:
   319→        error_msg = f"Error executing {function_name}: {str(e)}"
   320→        logger.error(error_msg)
   321→        return json.dumps({"error": error_msg}, ensure_ascii=False)
   322→
   323→
   324→# =============================================================================

112→    def dispatch(self, name: str, args: dict, **kwargs) -> str:
   113→        """Execute a tool handler by name.
   114→
   115→        * Async handlers are bridged automatically via ``_run_async()``.
   116→        * All exceptions are caught and returned as ``{"error": "..."}``
   117→          for consistent error format.
   118→        """
   119→        entry = self._tools.get(name)
   120→        if not entry:
   121→            return json.dumps({"error": f"Unknown tool: {name}"})
   122→        try:
   123→            if entry.is_async:
   124→                from model_tools import _run_async
   125→                return _run_async(entry.handler(args, **kwargs))
   126→            return entry.handler(args, **kwargs)
   127→        except Exception as e:
   128→            logger.exception("Tool %s dispatch error: %s", name, e)
   129→            return json.dumps({"error": f"Tool execution failed: {type(e).__name__}: {e}"})
   130→
   131→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   132→    # Query helpers  (replace redundant dicts in model_tools.py)
   133→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   134→
   135→    def get_all_tool_names(self) -> List[str]:
   136→        """Return sorted list of all registered tool names."""
   137→        return sorted(self._tools.keys())
   138→
   139→    def get_toolset_for_tool(self, name: str) -> Optional[str]:
   140→        """Return the toolset a tool belongs to, or None."""
   141→        entry = self._tools.get(name)
   142→        return entry.toolset if entry else None
   143→

## Implementation Plan: Unified Control Queue for AIAgent

### Overview

Replace `_pending_model_switch` (and future ad-hoc deferred state fields) with a thread-safe command queue drained at one well-defined point in the main loop. Fix tool registry handlers so `switch_model` and `smart_model` dispatch through normal registry paths instead of special-casing in `_invoke_tool`. Make the control API generic.

---

### File 1: `run_agent.py` -- AIAgent class

**A. Add the control queue (in `__init__`, around line 348)**

Replace:
```python
self._pending_model_switch = None  # dict with provider, model keys
```

With:
```python
import collections
self._control_queue = collections.deque()  # thread-safe command queue
self._control_lock = threading.Lock()      # for atomic drain
```

Add a dispatch table as a class-level or instance-level dict mapping command names to methods:

```python
self._control_dispatch = {
    "switch_model": self._handle_ctrl_switch_model,
}
```

**B. Add `enqueue_control` and `_drain_control_queue` methods**

```python
def enqueue_control(self, command: str, **params):
    """Enqueue a control command from any thread. Fire-and-forget."""
    self._control_queue.append({"command": command, **params})

def _drain_control_queue(self):
    """Process all queued control commands. Called from the loop thread only."""
    with self._control_lock:
        commands = []
        while self._control_queue:
            commands.append(self._control_queue.popleft())
    for cmd in commands:
        name = cmd.pop("command", None)
        handler = self._control_dispatch.get(name)
        if handler:
            try:
                handler(**cmd)
            except Exception as e:
                logging.error("Control command %s failed: %s", name, e)
        else:
            logging.warning("Unknown control command: %s", name)

def _handle_ctrl_switch_model(self, provider: str, model: str, **_extra):
    """Control-queue handler for switch_model."""
    self._switch_model(provider, model)
```

**C. Replace drain points (lines 4121-4124 and 4149-4152)**

Both blocks currently read:

    _ps = self._pending_model_switch
    self._pending_model_switch = None
    self._switch_model(_ps["provider"], _ps["model"])
```

Replace each with:
```python

```

There are two drain points: one before the main loop (line 4121) and one at the top of each iteration (line 4149). Both become identical one-liners.

**D. Fix `_invoke_tool` to remove switch_model/smart_model special cases (lines 3273-3297)**

Remove the entire `elif function_name == "switch_model"` and `elif function_name == "smart_model"` branches. The `else` branch at line 3298 already calls `handle_function_call`. But we need to pass `parent_agent=self` through. Change the `else` branch to:

    return handle_function_call(

The sequential path has inline dispatch for agent-loop tools. The `switch_model` and `smart_model` cases need to be removed from this duplicated if/elif chain. They will now flow through `handle_function_call` at lines 3652/3666, which needs to receive `parent_agent=self`:

    function_name, function_args, effective_task_id,

Keep this guard as-is (it falls through to `_execute_tool_calls_sequential` which will now dispatch via the registry).

### File 3: `tools/switch_model_tool.py`

Replace the stub lambda handler with a real one that extracts `parent_agent` from `**kw`:

```python
def _switch_model_handler(args, **kw):
    agent = kw.get("parent_agent")
    if agent is None:
        return json.dumps({"error": "switch_model requires agent context"})
    
    action = args.get("action", "list")
    if action == "list":
        return switch_model_list(
            current_provider=agent.provider,
            current_model=agent.model,
            original=agent._original_model,
        )
    else:
        return switch_model_switch(
            provider=args.get("provider", ""),
            model=args.get("model", ""),
            reason=args.get("reason", ""),
            agent=agent,
        )

registry.register(
    name="switch_model",
    toolset="switch_model",
    schema=SWITCH_MODEL_SCHEMA,
    handler=_switch_model_handler,
    check_fn=_check_switch_model,
)
```

### File 4: `tools/smart_model_tool.py`

Same pattern:

```python
def _smart_model_handler(args, **kw):
    agent = kw.get("parent_agent")
    if agent is None:
        return json.dumps({"error": "smart_model requires agent context"})
    
    return smart_model_handle(
        agent=agent,
        preset=args.get("preset", "balanced"),
        custom_provider=args.get("provider", ""),
        custom_model=args.get("model", ""),
    )

registry.register(
    name="smart_model",
    toolset="smart_model",
    schema=SMART_MODEL_SCHEMA,
    handler=_smart_model_handler,
    check_fn=_check_smart_model,
)
```

**B. Add the `control` handler**

```python
async def control(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    """Generic control endpoint. POST body: {"command": "...", ...params}"""
    [REDACTED]["key"]
    
    try:
        body = await request.json()
    except Exception:
        return _json_response({"error": "Invalid JSON body"}, status=400)
    
    command = body.get("command", "").strip()
    if not command:
        return _json_response({"error": "'command' is required"}, status=400)
    
    # Resolve _any
    if key == "_any":
        if not self.runner._running_agents:
            return _json_response({"error": "No running agents"}, status=404)
        key = next(iter(self.runner._running_agents))
    
    agent = self.runner._running_agents.get(key)
    if not agent:
        return _json_response(
            {"error": f"No running agent for session '{key}'"}, status=404,
        )
    
    params = {k: v for k, v in body.items() if k != "command"}
    
    logger.info("Control API: %s session=%s params=%s", command, key, params)
    
    agent.enqueue_control(command, **params)
    
    return _json_response({
        "success": True,
        "message": f"Command '{command}' queued",
    })
```

**C. Refactor `switch_model` to delegate to `control`**

Update the existing `switch_model` method to be a thin wrapper:

```python
async def switch_model(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    """Legacy endpoint — delegates to generic control."""
    # Rewrite as a control command for backward compat
    try:
        body = await request.json()
    except Exception:
        return _json_response({"error": "Invalid JSON body"}, status=400)
    
    provider = body.get("provider", "").strip()
    model = body.get("model", "").strip()
    if not provider or not model:
        return _json_response(
            {"error": "Both 'provider' and 'model' are required"}, status=400,
        )
    
    # Inject command name and forward
    body["command"] = "switch_model"
    # Mutate the request isn't clean, so just inline the logic:
    [REDACTED]["key"]
    if key == "_any":
        if not self.runner._running_agents:
            return _json_response({"error": "No running agents"}, status=404)
        key = next(iter(self.runner._running_agents))
    
    agent = self.runner._running_agents.get(key)
    if not agent:
        return _json_response(
            {"error": f"No running agent for session '{key}'"}, status=404,
        )
    
    agent.enqueue_control("switch_model", provider=provider, model=model)
    return _json_response({
        "success": True,
        "message": "Model switch queued — will apply before next API call",
        "target": {"provider": provider, "model": model},
    })
```

def control(
    self,
    command: str,
    session_key: str = "_any",
    **params,
) -> dict:
    """Send a generic control command to a running agent session."""
    return self._request("POST", f"/sessions/{session_key}/control", {
        "command": command,
        **params,
    })
```

**B. Update `switch_model` to use `control` internally (optional, for consistency)**

The existing `switch_model` method can remain as-is for backward compatibility since the server-side `/switch-model` endpoint still works. But it could optionally be updated to call the generic endpoint:

```python
def switch_model(self, provider, model, session_key="_any", reason=""):
    return self.control(
        "switch_model",
        session_key=session_key,
        provider=provider,
        model=model,
        reason=reason or "desloppify",
    )
```

### Sequencing and Dependencies

1. **Start with `tools/switch_model_tool.py` and `tools/smart_model_tool.py`** -- replace stub handlers with real ones that use `parent_agent` from `**kw`. These are self-contained changes.

2. **Update `model_tools.py`** -- remove `switch_model`/`smart_model` from `_AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS`, add `parent_agent` parameter, forward it in `registry.dispatch` calls.

3. **Update `run_agent.py`** in this order:
   - Add `_control_queue`, `_control_lock`, `_control_dispatch`, `enqueue_control`, `_drain_control_queue`, `_handle_ctrl_switch_model` 
   - Remove `_pending_model_switch` from `__init__`
   - Replace the two drain-point blocks with `self._drain_control_queue()`
   - Remove `switch_model`/`smart_model` branches from `_invoke_tool`
   - Add `parent_agent=self` to all `handle_function_call` calls
   - Remove `switch_model`/`smart_model` branches from `_execute_tool_calls_sequential`

4. **Update `gateway/control_api.py`** -- add generic `/control` route, refactor `switch_model` as alias.

5. **Update `gateway/hermes_control_client.py`** -- add `control()` method, optionally update `switch_model` to use it.

### Potential Challenges

1. **The sequential path duplication**: The `_execute_tool_calls_sequential` method at line 3480 has a giant if/elif chain that duplicates `_invoke_tool` but adds display logic (spinners, progress messages). The `switch_model` branch is not actually in the sequential display chain -- it was only in `_invoke_tool` which is called from the concurrent path. But `delegate_task` IS duplicated in both paths. Removing the switch_model/smart_model cases from the sequential path means they will fall through to the `else` branch which calls `handle_function_call`. This is correct: `handle_function_call` will now dispatch them through the registry, which calls the real handler with `parent_agent`.

2. **Thread safety of `_switch_model`**: When called from the control queue (drained on the loop thread), `_switch_model` runs on the loop thread, same as when called via tool dispatch. No concurrency issue. When called from the control API without the queue (the old `hasattr` fallback), it ran on the aiohttp thread. The new design always goes through the queue, eliminating this race.

3. **The `_original_model` access in `switch_model_list`**: The handler now gets `agent._original_model` through `parent_agent`. This attribute is set in `__init__` and never changes, so it is safe to read from any path.

4. **Backward compatibility**: The `/switch-model` endpoint continues to work, just routes through `enqueue_control` internally. External clients using `HermesControl.switch_model()` see no change. The response shape changes slightly (loses the `"current"` field since we now always queue), but callers should already handle the queued case.
